<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> We prove Campana's orbifold Iitaka subadditivity conjecture for rational simple normal crossing boundaries on compact manifolds in Fujiki class $`\mathcal C`$, including coefficient one. Ordinary and logarithmic subadditivity follow.

- **claim-orbifold-subadditivity** (theorem, [source](#stmt-main-orbifold)): Let $X$ be a smooth compact connected complex manifold in Fujiki class $\mathcal C$, let $\Delta$ be a rational SNC boundary, and let $f:X\to Y$ be a surjective holomorphic map with connected fibers onto a normal compact irreducible complex space. For a very general smooth fiber $F$, put $\Delta_F=\Delta\|_F$. Then $$\label{main:inequality} \kappa(X,K_X+\Delta) \geq\kappa(F,K_F+\Delta_F)+\kappa(f\mid\Delta).$$
  - Explanation: The total log Kodaira dimension is bounded below by the fiber contribution plus the invariant orbifold base contribution, including boundaries with coefficient one.
- **claim-logarithmic-modification-invariance** (theorem, [source](#stmt-setup-log-modification)): Let $p:Z'\to Z$ be a proper bimeromorphic holomorphic map between smooth compact manifolds. Suppose that $\Gamma$ has simple normal crossing support and coefficients in $\mathbf Q\cap[0,1]$, and set $$\Gamma'=p_*^{-1}\Gamma+\operatorname{Exc}(p)_{\mathrm{red}}.$$ Assume, after further resolution if necessary, that this boundary has simple normal crossing support. There is an effective $p$-exceptional rational divisor $A$ such that $$\label{setup:log-discrepancy} K_{Z'}+\Gamma'=p^*(K_Z+\Gamma)+A.$$ For every sufficiently divisible positive integer $m$, the natural identification of meromorphic pluricanonical forms induces $$\label{setup:section-invariance} p_*\mathcal O_{Z'}\bigl(m(K_{Z'}+\Gamma')\bigr) =\mathcal O_Z\bigl(m(K_Z+\Gamma)\bigr).$$ These identifications respect multiplication, so the divisible section rings and their Iitaka dimensions agree. Moreover, $p$ satisfies <a href="#setup:orbifold-morphism" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="setup:orbifold-morphism">[setup:orbifold-morphism]</a>.
  - Explanation: Adding the reduced exceptional boundary preserves the divisible log section ring and gives an effective exceptional discrepancy.
- **claim-exceptional-center-descent** (theorem, [source](#stmt-setup-exceptional-descent)): In the situation of [setup:log-modification](#setup:log-modification), a meromorphic $m$-pluricanonical form on $Z'$ satisfying the boundary bounds of $m\Gamma'$ at every nonexceptional prime descends to a section of $m(K_Z+\Gamma)$ whenever $m\Gamma$ and $m\Gamma'$ are integral. The assertion also holds after tensoring by the pullback of a line bundle on $Z$.
  - Explanation: Boundary bounds at nonexceptional primes suffice for descent to the original smooth pair, also with a pulled-back line bundle.
- **claim-fixed-base-and-fiber-compatibility** (theorem, [source](#stmt-setup-modification-compatibility)): Let $p:(Z',\Gamma')\to(Z,\Gamma)$ be a modification with the boundary rule of [setup:log-modification](#setup:log-modification). Then:* 1. *If $p$ is a modification over a fixed base, it induces a logarithmic modification on a very general smooth fiber. The log Kodaira dimensions of the corresponding fibers agree.* 2. *A finite composition of such modifications again has boundary equal to the strict transform of the initial boundary plus the reduced exceptional divisor of the composite.* 3. *If $g:(Z,\Gamma)\to T$ has smooth base and $g'=g\circ p$, then $$\label{setup:fixed-base-boundary} B(g',\Gamma')=B(g,\Gamma).$$
  - Explanation: The prescribed boundary rule is compatible with fiber restriction and composition, and preserves the inf-multiplicity boundary over a fixed smooth base.
- **claim-flattened-exceptional-reference** (construction, [source](#stmt-setup-flattening)): Let $g_0:(Z_0,\Gamma_0)\to T_0$ be a fibration from a smooth compact pair with rational SNC boundary in $[0,1]$ to a smooth compact manifold. There are a smooth-base modification $q:T\to T_0$, a smooth-source modification $p:Z\to Z_0$, and a fibration $g:Z\to T$ with $q\circ g=g_0\circ p$ such that, for $$\Gamma=p_*^{-1}\Gamma_0+\operatorname{Exc}(p)_{\mathrm{red}},$$ the boundary has simple normal crossing support and every prime divisor $E\subset Z$ satisfying $\operatorname{codim}_Tg(E)\geq2$ is $p$-exceptional. In particular every such prime has coefficient one in $\Gamma$, and the resulting fibration is neat with reference pair $(Z_0,\Gamma_0)$.* *One may resolve prescribed proper analytic bad loci on the base during this construction. After any finite sequence of further base modifications, one can choose dominating smooth source models for which the same exceptional assertion holds with respect to the original reference $Z_0$.
  - Explanation: Flattening before source resolution makes every divisor contracted into higher base codimension exceptional over the fixed reference source. This control persists through further base modifications.
- **claim-simultaneous-model-control** (theorem, [source](#stmt-setup-simultaneous)): Suppose a smooth pair $(X_1,\Delta_1)$ has a fibration $f_1:X_1\to Y$ to a fixed smooth base, and every prime divisor contracted by $f_1$ into codimension at least two has coefficient one in $\Delta_1$. Put $C=B(f_1,\Delta_1)$. Let a factorization through another smooth compact space be given, after an allowed source modification if necessary, by $$X_1\xrightarrow{g_1}W_1\xrightarrow{h_1}Y.$$ Apply [setup:flattening](#setup:flattening) to $g_1$ and make any finite number of subsequent modifications of $W_1$, always taking dominating smooth source models with reduced added exceptional boundaries. The resulting diagram $X\xrightarrow{g}W\xrightarrow{h}Y$, with source boundary $\Delta$, can be chosen to have all the following properties:* 1. *Every prime contracted by $g$ into codimension at least two is exceptional over the chosen smooth reference source for $g_1$.* 2. *Every prime contracted by $f=h\circ g$ into codimension at least two in $Y$ has coefficient one in $\Delta$.* 3. *$B(f,\Delta)=C$. In particular, for each prime $D\subset Y$ and each prime $E\subset X$ dominating $D$, $$\label{setup:retained-multiplicity} \mathop{\mathrm{ord}}_E(f^*D)\,m_\Delta(E)\geq m_C(D).$$* 4. *The log Kodaira dimensions of the total source and of its very general fibers over $Y$ are unchanged by these source modifications.* *The hypothesis on contracted divisors follows from [setup:flattening](#setup:flattening), applied to the original map to its resolved base before fixing $Y$.
  - Explanation: Intermediate-base changes can retain both the exceptional reference needed for descent and the original base boundary, without changing the total or fiber log Kodaira dimensions.
- **claim-relative-log-iitaka-reduction** (construction, [source](#stmt-application-relative-iitaka)): Let $f:(X,\Delta)\to Y$ be a fibration between smooth compact manifolds in Fujiki class $\mathcal C$, with $\Delta$ a rational SNC boundary in $[0,1]$. Suppose that $$k=\kappa(F,K_F+\Delta_F)\geq 0$$ on very general smooth fibers. After modifications of the source, adding the reduced exceptional divisor to the strict transform of the boundary, there is a factorization $$X\xrightarrow{g}W\xrightarrow{h}Y$$ with smooth compact intermediate space in class $\mathcal C$, both maps proper with connected fibers, and $$\dim W_y=k,\qquad \kappa(G,K_G+\Delta_G)=0$$ for very general fibers of $h$ and $g$, respectively. The factorization restricts to the log Iitaka fibration on very general $f$-fibers. These properties persist on higher models obtained by the same rule for the source boundary.
  - Explanation: The relative log Iitaka factorization separates the original fiber Kodaira dimension from fibers of log Kodaira dimension zero, with persistence under the allowed source modifications.
- **claim-rank-one-section-comparison** (theorem, [source](#stmt-rankone-comparison)): Let $g:(X,\Delta)\to W$ be a fibration with connected fibers from a smooth compact Kähler manifold to a smooth compact manifold in class $\mathcal C$. Suppose that $\Delta$ is an SNC boundary with rational coefficients in $[0,1]$ and that $\kappa(G,K_G+\Delta_G)=0$ on a very general smooth fiber $G$. After the modifications of [setup:log-modification](#setup:log-modification), [setup:flattening](#setup:flattening), fix a smooth compact reference pair $(X_0,\Delta_0)$ and a modification $$\pi:X\longrightarrow X_0,\qquad \Delta=\pi_*^{-1}\Delta_0+\operatorname{Exc}(\pi)_{\mathrm{red}},$$ such that every prime divisor mapped by $g$ into codimension at least two is $\pi$-exceptional. Then there are a rational line bundle $M$ and a rational divisor $T$ on $W$ with the following properties.* 1. *A positive integral multiple of $M$ is the parabolic highest Hodge line of a complex direct summand of a pure, real-polarizable variation with an integral lattice on a dense open subset of $W$. Its parabolic extension is the line to which the adjoint-positivity theorem, [period:adjoint](#period:adjoint), will apply.* 2. *The divisor $T$ is supported on an SNC divisor and satisfies $$\label{rankone:boundary} B(g,\Delta)\leq T\leq 1.$$ In particular, $T$ is effective. If every prime over a prime $D$ of $W$ has $\Delta$-coefficient one, then $T_D=1$.* 3. *In sufficiently divisible degrees $q$, multiplication by the relative generating forms gives linear isomorphisms $$\label{rankone:sections} H^0\bigl(W,q(K_W+T+M)\bigr) \simeq H^0\bigl(X,q(K_X+\Delta)\bigr) \simeq H^0\bigl(X_0,q(K_{X_0}+\Delta_0)\bigr).$$ In a common divisible grading they preserve products and ratios of sections, and hence the dimensions of the images of their linear systems.* 4. *The same comparison holds relatively. If $h:W\to Y$ and $f_0:X_0\to Y$ are holomorphic maps with $f_0\pi=hg$, then $$\label{rankone:relative-sections} h_*\mathcal O_W\bigl(q(K_W+T+M)\bigr) \simeq (f_0)_*\mathcal O_{X_0}\bigl(q(K_{X_0}+\Delta_0)\bigr)$$ for sufficiently divisible $q$. The comparison of ratios also applies to systems over $Y$ and to their restrictions wherever generic base change holds. The relative identity remains valid after tensoring by a line bundle on $Y$, or a rational line bundle in degrees clearing its denominator.
  - Explanation: The root Hodge line and its boundary correction identify complete divisible section spaces on the base and the fixed original source. The identification preserves products and ratios and also holds relatively.
- **claim-root-character-top-line** (theorem, [source](#stmt-rankone-eigenspace)): The tautological form spans the root-character part of $F^dH^d(\widetilde G\setminus H,\mathbf C)$ on a general fiber of the cyclic cover. In particular, that part has dimension one.
  - Explanation: The tautological root form spans its character part of the highest Hodge step; this does not assert that the entire highest step is a line.
- **claim-tensor-descent-of-hodge-lines** (construction, [source](#stmt-rankone-descent)): A positive tensor power of these local Hodge lines is the highest Hodge line of a complex direct summand of a global real-polarizable integral pure variation on $W^\circ$.
  - Explanation: A positive tensor power removes the deck-transformation ambiguity and realizes the local lines inside a global integral pure variation.
- **claim-pure-weight-extension-identification** (theorem, [source](#stmt-rankone-extension)): The projection of the rank-one top eigenspace to its nonzero pure weight grade is an isomorphism on canonical extensions after unipotentization. Consequently it is an isomorphism of parabolic rational lines before that base change.
  - Explanation: Projection to the unique nonzero pure weight grade preserves the canonical extension of the highest character line, and hence its parabolic extension.
- **claim-composition-of-inf-multiplicities** (theorem, [source](#stmt-application-composition)): Suppose that $X\xrightarrow{g}W\xrightarrow{h}Y$ is a diagram of surjective maps between smooth spaces, and $\Delta,T,C$ are rational boundaries on these spaces. If $$T\geq B(g,\Delta),\qquad C\leq B(h\circ g,\Delta),$$ then $B(h,T)\geq C$. If, in addition, every prime divisor of $X$ mapped into codimension at least two in $Y$ has coefficient one in $\Delta$, and $T\leq1$, then every prime divisor of $W$ mapped into codimension at least two in $Y$ has coefficient one in $T$.
  - Explanation: The intermediate orbifold boundary dominates the prescribed original base boundary. The additional contracted-divisor hypothesis propagates coefficient one to the intermediate source.
- **claim-hodge-line-adjoint-positivity** (theorem, [source](#stmt-period-adjoint)): Let $B$ be a smooth compact connected manifold in class $\mathcal C$ and $B^\circ\subset B$ a dense Zariski open set with SNC complement. Let $\mathbb V$ be a pure real-polarizable variation of Hodge structures on $B^\circ$ with an integral ambient lattice. Suppose a complex direct summand of $\mathbb V_{\mathbf C}$ has a rank-one highest nonzero Hodge step, with parabolic rational extension $L$. There are a modification $\tau:B'\to B$, a surjective morphism $p:B'\to S$ with connected fibers to a smooth projective variety, and a nef rational line bundle $P$ on $S$ such that $$\label{period:conclusion} \tau^*L=p^*P,\qquad K_S+aP\text{ is big for some }a\in\mathbf Q_{>0}.$$ Positive rational rescaling of $L$ is allowed, and the line identity persists under further modifications. If $S$ is a point, $\tau^*L$ is rationally trivial.
  - Explanation: After modification, the extreme Hodge line descends to a nef rational line on a projective quotient whose canonical divisor becomes big after a positive rational twist.
- **claim-log-general-type-fiber-addition** (theorem, [source](#stmt-generaltype-addition)): Let $h\colon V\to Y$ be a surjective holomorphic map with connected fibers between smooth compact complex manifolds in Fujiki class $\mathcal C$. Let $T$ and $C$ be effective rational divisors with simple normal crossing support and coefficients in $[0,1]$ on $V$ and $Y$, respectively. Suppose that* 1. *$K_F+T_F$ is big for a very general smooth fiber $F$ of $h$;* 2. *$C\leq B(h,T)$;* 3. *$T_E=1$ for every prime divisor $E$ of $V$ whose image in $Y$ has codimension at least two.* *Then $$\label{generaltype:inequality} \kappa(V,K_V+T)\ \geq\ \dim F+\kappa(Y,K_Y+C).$$ As usual, the assertion is automatic if the last term is $-\infty$.
  - Explanation: A big fiber log canonical divisor supplies the fiber dimension in the lower bound, provided the inf-multiplicity and contracted-divisor boundary conditions hold.
- **claim-big-base-twist-effectivity** (theorem, [source](#stmt-application-weak-effectivity)): Let $p:(W,T)\to S$ be surjective, with $W$ smooth compact in class $\mathcal C$, $S$ smooth projective, and $T$ a rational SNC boundary. Suppose one fixed divisible relative log pluricanonical system is nonzero on very general fibers. For every big rational line bundle $H$ on $S$, the rational line bundle $K_{W/S}+T+p^*H$ has a nonzero section in some positive degree.
  - Explanation: Fiberwise nonvanishing and weak positivity give a global nonzero section after any big rational base twist, in an unspecified positive degree.
- **claim-adjoint-addition-by-section-cancellation** (theorem, [source](#stmt-addition-principle)): Let $h:W\to Y$ be a fibration between smooth compact manifolds in class $\mathcal C$. Let $T,C$ be rational SNC boundaries with $C\leq B(h,T)$, and suppose every prime of $W$ whose image has codimension at least two in $Y$ has coefficient one in $T$. Let $p:W\to S$ be a surjective morphism with connected fibers to a smooth projective variety. Suppose $P$ is a nef rational line bundle on $S$, $M=p^*P$, and $K_S+a_0P$ is big for some rational $a_0>0$. If $K_W+T+M$ is big on very general $h$-fibers, then $$\kappa(W,K_W+T+M) \geq\dim(W/Y)+\kappa(Y,K_Y+C).$$
  - Explanation: Adjoint positivity and fiberwise bigness yield subadditivity with the nef moduli term. Multiplication by a fixed section cancels the ample perturbation while retaining section ratios.
- **claim-curvature-rank-and-numerical-dimension** (theorem, [source](#stmt-period-numerics)): Let $T$ be a smooth compact Kähler manifold, let $E\subset T$ be a simple normal crossing divisor, and let $A$ be the parabolic extension of the rank-one highest step of a polarizable pure complex variation on $T\setminus E$ with quasi-unipotent local monodromy. Then $A$ is nef. Its numerical dimension equals the generic rank of the map that remembers this line in a flat trivialization.* *The corresponding assertion for the Griffiths line, the tensor product of the determinants of the Hodge filtration steps, uses the full period map. In particular, its rational extension is big if the full period map is generically immersive.
  - Explanation: The parabolic highest line is nef, and its numerical dimension measures the line map. The analogous Griffiths-line statement measures the full period map instead.
- **claim-boundary-graded-line-restriction** (theorem, [source](#stmt-period-boundary)): In the unipotent case of [period:numerics](#period:numerics), let $E_i$ be a smooth boundary component and $N_i$ its monodromy logarithm. On the open stratum of $E_i$, the limiting highest line occurs in a unique weight grade of the limiting mixed variation. The restriction $A\|_{E_i}$ is the Schmid extension, across the remaining boundary of $E_i$, of that graded Hodge line. Its numerical dimension is therefore the generic rank of the graded line map.
  - Explanation: In the unipotent setting, restriction to a boundary component agrees with the extension of the unique limiting graded highest line, so its numerical dimension is a graded line-map rank.
- **claim-rational-adjoint-reduction** (construction, [source](#stmt-period-adjoint-reduction)): In the setting of [period:adjoint](#period:adjoint), let $G$ be the identity component of the rational Zariski closure of monodromy. Then $G$ is semisimple. Its adjoint local system is a rationally polarized integral variation of weight zero.* *There is a monodromy-invariant collection of rational simple adjoint factors, each containing a complex factor acting nontrivially on the highest-line orbit, with the following properties. The resulting adjoint variation $\mathbb A$ has discrete integral monodromy. After a positive power and up to a constant one-dimensional complex Hodge shift, the original line is the highest line of a complex subvariation of a tensor construction on $\mathbb A$. On a finite cover preserving the factors, that line splits into the tensor product of the highest lines belonging to the individual complex factors. All these highest steps have rank one.
  - Explanation: Whole rational adjoint factors retain discrete integral monodromy and recover a positive power of the chosen complex line through a tensor construction, including scalar monodromy.
- **claim-generic-adjoint-period-stabilizer** (theorem, [source](#stmt-period-stabilizer)): Let $G$ be a connected semisimple adjoint group over $\mathbf Q$, and let $\mathbb A_{\mathbf Q}$ be a rationally polarized integral variation whose fiber is $\mathfrak g_{\mathbf Q}=\operatorname{Lie}(G)$ and whose connected algebraic monodromy is $G$, acting by its adjoint representation. No nonidentity rational Lie algebra automorphism of $\mathfrak g_{\mathbf Q}$ fixes all generic lifted periods of $\mathbb A$. The automorphism need not belong to the monodromy group.
  - Explanation: A rational Lie algebra automorphism fixing the generic lifted adjoint periods must be the identity, even when it is outside the monodromy group.
- **claim-projective-period-quotient** (construction, [source](#stmt-period-quotient)): In [period:adjoint](#period:adjoint), after modifications there is a map $p:B'\to S$ with connected fibers to a smooth projective variety and a nef rational line $P$ with $\tau^*L=p^*P$. On a dense open subset $S^\circ$, the retained adjoint variation of [period:adjoint-reduction](#period:adjoint-reduction) descends, has the same connected algebraic monodromy, and has a generically immersive period map. The line $P$ is its parabolically extended highest-weight line, up to the positive scaling already described. If the retained collection is empty, $L$ is rationally trivial and $S$ may be a point.
  - Explanation: The retained full periods have a smooth projective connected-fiber quotient. Their highest-weight line descends with its exact parabolic extension.
- **claim-marked-boundary-rank-loss** (bound, [source](#stmt-period-rankloss)): For every component $D_i$ of $D$, $$\label{period:strictdrop} \nu(P\|_{D_i})<\nu(P).$$
  - Explanation: Every marked component with nonidentity local monodromy has strictly smaller numerical dimension for the restricted nef line.
- **claim-boundary-subtraction-by-section-growth** (theorem, [source](#stmt-period-subtract)): Let $Z$ be a smooth projective variety, $J$ a nef rational divisor, $A$ a rational divisor, and $D=\sum_{i=1}^s d_iE_i$ an effective rational divisor with smooth prime components. Suppose $A+D$ is big and $$\nu(J\|_{E_i})<r:=\nu(J) \quad\text{for every component of }D.$$ Then $A+tJ$ is big for every sufficiently large rational $t$.
  - Explanation: A nef direction with strictly smaller numerical dimension on each effective boundary component eventually compensates for removing that boundary from a big divisor.
- **claim-analytic-constructible-selection** (theorem, [source](#stmt-generaltype-selection)): Let $Y$ be an irreducible compact complex space. For each positive integer $j$, suppose that $q_j\colon Q_j\to Y$ is a proper surjective map from an irreducible compact complex space and that, over a dense analytic open $U_j\subset Y$, its fibers are irreducible of a fixed dimension $r_j$. Let $$\phi_j\colon Q_j\dashrightarrow Z_j$$ be a meromorphic map with irreducible projective image $Z_j$. Suppose that a dense open part of $q_j^{-1}(U_j)$, called the genuine frame locus, meets every fiber densely and that $\phi_j$ is holomorphic there. Let $G_j\subset Z_j$ be a countable union of algebraically constructible subsets.* *Let $\Lambda_j\subset U_j$ be sets whose union contains a very general subset of $Y$, such that for $y\in\Lambda_j$ all genuine frames above $y$ map into $G_j$. Then some $\Lambda_j$ is not contained in a countable union of proper closed analytic subsets of $Y$, and for that index one of the constructible subsets making up $G_j$ is dense in $Z_j$. In particular $G_j$ contains a nonempty algebraic open subset of $Z_j$.
  - Explanation: A countable collection covering very general parameters contains an index with dense constructible good data, producing a nonempty algebraic open in the corresponding image.
- **claim-analytic-stable-family-comparison** (construction, [source](#stmt-generaltype-stable-comparison)): Let $h\colon V\to Y$ be a surjective holomorphic map with connected fibers, where $V$ is a smooth compact Kähler manifold and $Y$ is a smooth compact manifold in class $\mathcal C$. Let $A$ be an effective rational SNC divisor on $V$, horizontal over $Y$, with coefficients strictly less than one. Suppose $K_F+A_F$ is big on very general fibers $F$ of positive dimension. There are* 1. *a proper generically finite map $\tau\colon Y'\to Y$, with $Y'$ smooth, compact, and in class $\mathcal C$;* 2. *a surjective holomorphic map $\rho\colon Y'\to R$, with $R$ smooth and projective;* 3. *a projective stable family $\pi\colon(\mathcal V,\mathcal A)\to R$ of maximal variation, with klt general fiber;* *such that the main component of $V\times_Y Y'$ is bimeromorphic over $Y'$ to $\mathcal V\times_RY'$. Over a dense analytic open, this meromorphic comparison is the fiberwise log canonical model map for $K_F+A_F$, with the horizontal pushforward of $A$ as boundary. The space $R$ may be a point.
  - Explanation: A generically finite analytic base cover compares the original fiberwise log canonical models with a projective stable family of maximal variation and klt general fiber.
- **claim-stable-family-valuation-bounds** (theorem, [source](#stmt-generaltype-stable-orders)): In the situation of [generaltype:stable-comparison](#generaltype:stable-comparison), pull back a holomorphic section of a divisible multiple of $K_{\mathcal V/R}+\mathcal A$, and compare it with relative pluriforms on the main component of $V\times_Y Y'$. It satisfies the horizontal bound given by the pullback of $A$. Moreover, on a common resolution it satisfies [generaltype:relative-log-bound](#generaltype:relative-log-bound) at every prime lying over a divisor of any smooth modification of the comparison base.
  - Explanation: Relative sections pulled back from the stable family satisfy both the horizontal boundary bound and the vertical logarithmic bound, including divisors exposed on higher base models.
- **claim-descended-relative-pluriform-system** (construction, [source](#stmt-generaltype-relative-system)): Under the hypotheses of [generaltype:stable-comparison](#generaltype:stable-comparison), there is a positive integer $b_0$ and a finite-dimensional space $$\mathcal S\subset H^0_{\mathrm{mer}}\bigl(V,\mathcal O_V(b_0K_{V/Y})\bigr)$$ whose associated meromorphic map has rank $\dim F$ on general $h$-fibers, with the following properties:* 1. *every member has horizontal pole bound $b_0A$;* 2. *every member satisfies the relative logarithmic bound at primes over base divisors;* 3. *the same bound may be tested after any modification of the base, at a divisor exposed on that modification.* *The same assertions hold for the systems of products of members of $\mathcal S$.
  - Explanation: Invariant descent produces a finite-dimensional relative pluriform system of full fiber rank, with pole bounds stable under base modification and products.
- **claim-expose-vertical-divisor-on-base** (construction, [source](#stmt-generaltype-expose-valuation)): Let $h\colon V\to Y$ be a proper surjective holomorphic map of irreducible compact complex spaces, with $Y$ smooth. If a prime divisor $E$ of a smooth $V$ does not dominate $Y$, there is a modification of the base and a dominating main-component model on which the strict transform of $E$ maps onto a prime divisor of the modified base.
  - Explanation: A vertical source divisor can be made to dominate a prime on a modified base, allowing its existing valuation to be checked by a base-divisor order test.
- **claim-prescribed-logarithmic-base-comparison** (bound, [source](#stmt-spec-base-comparison)): Let $f:(X,\Delta)\to Y$ be a fibration of smooth compact complex manifolds, where $\Delta$ has rational coefficients in $[0,1]$ and simple normal crossing support. Let $D_Y$ be a reduced simple normal crossing divisor on $Y$. Suppose that every prime component of $f^*D_Y$ has coefficient one in $\Delta$. Then $$\kappa(f\mid\Delta)\geq\kappa(Y,K_Y+D_Y).$$
  - Explanation: A prescribed reduced logarithmic base contributes a lower bound for the invariant base dimension when all primes in its pullback have source coefficient one.
- **claim-logarithmic-subadditivity** (theorem, [source](#stmt-spec-logarithmic)): Let $f:X\to Y$ be a surjective morphism with connected fibers between smooth connected projective complex varieties. Let $D_X,D_Y$ be reduced simple normal crossing divisors, with the zero divisor allowed, such that $$\operatorname{Supp}(f^*D_Y)\subseteq\operatorname{Supp}(D_X).$$ For a very general fiber $F$, put $D_F=D_X\|_F$. Then $$\label{spec:log-inequality} \kappa(X,K_X+D_X) \geq\kappa(F,K_F+D_F)+\kappa(Y,K_Y+D_Y).$$ The same statement holds for compact manifolds in Fujiki class $\mathcal C$ and holomorphic fibrations between them.
  - Explanation: Reduced SNC pairs satisfying the pullback-support condition obey logarithmic subadditivity, both projectively and for compact class-C holomorphic fibrations.
- **claim-characteristic-zero-subadditivity** (theorem, [source](#stmt-spec-ordinary)): Let $k$ be an algebraically closed field of characteristic zero, and let $f:X\to Y$ be a surjective morphism with connected fibers between smooth connected projective $k$-varieties. If $F$ is its geometric generic fiber, then $$\label{spec:ordinary-inequality} \kappa(X)\geq\kappa(F)+\kappa(Y).$$ More generally, the logarithmic inequality of Corollary <a href="#spec:logarithmic" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="spec:logarithmic">31</a>, with the same boundary hypotheses, holds over $k$, with the geometric generic fiber in place of a very general complex fiber.
  - Explanation: Ordinary and logarithmic subadditivity extend to algebraically closed fields of characteristic zero with geometric generic fibers.
- **claim-core-kodaira-additivity** (theorem, [source](#stmt-spec-core-dimension)): Let $(X,\Delta)$ be a smooth compact orbifold pair in Fujiki class $\mathcal C$, with rational simple normal crossing boundary coefficients in $[0,1]$. Let $C=C(X,\Delta)$ be its core base and $(G,\Delta_G)$ a very general orbifold fiber. Then $$\label{spec:core-equality} \kappa(X,K_X+\Delta) =\kappa(G,K_G+\Delta_G)+\dim C.$$ If $\kappa(X,K_X+\Delta)\geq0$, then $$0\leq\dim C\leq\kappa(X,K_X+\Delta),$$ and equality in the second inequality holds precisely when $\kappa(G,K_G+\Delta_G)=0$. In particular, $\kappa(X,K_X+\Delta)=0$ implies that $(X,\Delta)$ is special.
  - Explanation: The core gives equality between the total log Kodaira dimension and the fiber contribution plus core dimension. Log Kodaira dimension zero implies specialness; the converse is not asserted.
- **claim-core-decomposition-tower** (construction, [source](#stmt-spec-core-tower)): Let $(X,\Delta)$ be as in Corollary <a href="#spec:core-dimension" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="spec:core-dimension">33</a>, and set $n=\dim X$. Starting from $(X,\Delta)$, successively take the $\kappa$-rational quotient $r$ and the Moishezon–Iitaka fibration $M$ of its stable orbifold base. At each iteration choose such compatible representatives and equip the new base with the stable inf-multiplicity orbifold structure of the composite. The composite after at most $n$ iterations is the core, up to bimeromorphic equivalence of fibrations: $$c_{(X,\Delta)}=(M\circ r)^n.$$ The positive-dimensional very general stable orbifold fibers of the $r$-steps have $\kappa_+=-\infty$, and those of the $M$-steps have Kodaira dimension zero. In particular, for a special pair this tower ends at a point.
  - Explanation: Alternating the rational quotient and Iitaka construction on compatible stable orbifold bases reaches the core within the source-dimension bound.
- **claim-reduced-minimal-root-cover** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-reduced-cyclic-top)): Let $(J,D)$ be a smooth geometrically integral projective pair over a field $K$ of characteristic zero. Assume that $D$ is reduced and has simple normal crossings and that $\kappa(J_{\overline K},K_{J_{\overline K}}+D_{\overline K})=0$. Choose the least positive integer $p$ for which $H^0(J,p(K_J+D))\ne0$, a nonzero section $\omega$ in that space, and put $$E=\operatorname{div}(\omega)+pD, \qquad \Delta=D-\frac1pE.$$ Write $\omega=b\eta^{\otimes p}$ in a rational canonical frame, and let $\tau:N\to J$ be the normalization in $K(J)[t]/(t^p-b)$. Let $\rho:\widetilde N\to N$ be a log resolution, and set $$P=\operatorname{Supp}(\Delta^{=1}),\qquad D_{\widetilde N}=\bigl((\tau\circ\rho)^{-1}P\bigr)_{\mathrm{red}}.$$ Then $N$ is geometrically integral, the rational top form $\Omega=t\tau^*\eta$ extends logarithmically to $(\widetilde N,D_{\widetilde N})$, and $$H^0\bigl(\widetilde N,m(K_{\widetilde N}+D_{\widetilde N})\bigr) =K\,\Omega^m\qquad(m\ge1).$$
  - Explanation: For a reduced log Kodaira-zero pair, the minimal root cover is geometrically integral and its entire logarithmic pluriform space is generated by powers of the root form.
- **claim-integral-pure-moduli-realization** (construction, [source](#stmt-cor-reduced-integral-moduli)): Let $x:X\to W$ be a projective fibration between smooth complex projective varieties, with geometrically integral generic fiber $J$. Let $D_X$ be a reduced SNC boundary whose restriction $D_J$ satisfies the hypotheses of Lemma <a href="#lem:reduced-cyclic-top" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:reduced-cyclic-top">35</a>. Let $M_W$ be the rational Hodge divisor of the rank-one reduction, with its rational frame and parabolic orders as in Equation <a href="#rankone:order" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="rankone:order">[rankone:order]</a>. On sufficiently high smooth models, there is a polarizable integral pure variation of Hodge structures on a dense smooth open $W^\circ\subset W$ whose top nonzero Hodge filtration piece is a line, and $$M_W\sim_{\mathbf Q} \text{the rational canonical extension of that line}.$$ In the canonical-bundle-formula normalization, this is the moduli divisor of the auxiliary generic subpair $\Delta_J=D_J-E_J/p$, which may have negative coefficients.
  - Explanation: The entire highest line of an integral pure variation realizes the rational Hodge divisor with its exact normalization, also identifying the moduli divisor of an auxiliary subpair that may be negative.
- **claim-threshold-normalization-and-simple-component** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-rankone-normalization)): Let $x:X'\to W$ be a projective fibration on smooth models, let $D'$ be an effective reduced SNC divisor, and suppose that its geometric generic fiber $(J,D_J)$ has logarithmic Kodaira dimension zero. Fix a nonzero relative rational $p$-canonical form $s$ generating $H^0(J,p(K_J+D_J))$. At a prime $P$ of $W$ set $$a_E=\operatorname{ord}_E(x^*P),\qquad r_E=p^{-1}\operatorname{ord}^{pK_{X'/W}}_E(s),\qquad d_E=\operatorname{coeff}_E(D'),\qquad t_P=\min_{E\mapsto P}\frac{r_E+d_E}{a_E}.$$ Here the minimum is over actual components on the chosen source model. Choose that model so that the generator divisor, boundary and inverse image of $P$ have SNC support over the generic point of $P$; later source modifications carry strict boundary plus reduced exceptional boundary. The coefficients in the rank-one comparison satisfy $$\alpha_P=t_P,\qquad \beta_P=\inf_{E\mapsto P}\frac{1+r_E}{a_E}-1,\qquad T_P=1-c_P, \quad c_P:=\inf_{E\mapsto P}\frac{1+r_E}{a_E}-t_P.$$ The infima allow divisorial valuations on higher smooth source models. Equivalently, $c_P$ is the log canonical threshold of $x^*P$ for the normalized auxiliary subpair $$\Delta^{\mathrm{aux}} =-p^{-1}\operatorname{div}_{K_{X'/W}}(s)+x^*\sum_Qt_QQ.$$ If $T_P=0$, an actual component attaining $t_P$ has $a_E=1$, $d_E=0$, and attains the threshold infimum as well.
  - Explanation: The boundary correction is the complement of the normalized auxiliary log canonical threshold. A vanishing boundary coefficient forces an actual minimizing component of multiplicity one outside the source boundary.
- **claim-permitted-normalized-base-models** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-normalized-models)): Suppose that $(W,T)$ is smooth with effective SNC rational boundary and that the moduli divisor $M$ is a rational Cartier divisor on $W$. For a smooth modification $\mu:W'\to W$, put $$T'=\mu_*^{-1}T+\operatorname{Exc}(\mu)_{\mathrm{red}}, \qquad M'=\mu^*M,$$ and assume the resulting boundary is SNC. Then pullback identifies the section spaces of every sufficiently divisible multiple of $K_W+T+M$ and $K_{W'}+T'+M'$. At unchanged codimension-one points the normalization in Lemma <a href="#lem:rankone-normalization" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:rankone-normalization">38</a> is preserved. Every new exceptional prime has coefficient one in $T'$.
  - Explanation: Strict boundary plus reduced exceptional boundary, together with pullback of the moduli divisor, preserves divisible adjoint sections and unchanged divisorial normalization.
- **claim-divisorial-section-and-galois-test** (theorem, [source](#stmt-p1logcbfalt-section-test)): Let $V$ be normal and $D$ an integral Weil divisor. A rational section belongs to $H^0(V,D)$ if and only if it satisfies the corresponding order inequality at every prime divisor of $V$. If $\pi:V'\to V$ is finite surjective with $V'$ normal and $D$ is Cartier, this test on $V'$ needs only primes lying over prime divisors of $V$. If $\pi$ is Galois, then $$H^0(V',\pi^*D)^{\operatorname{Gal}(V'/V)}=H^0(V,D).$$
  - Explanation: Normality reduces section membership to divisorial order inequalities. Finite covers preserve the relevant divisorial centers, and Galois invariants recover downstairs sections.
- **claim-generically-finite-iitaka-invariance** (theorem, [source](#stmt-p1logcbfalt-finite)): If $\pi:V'\to V$ is a dominant generically finite morphism of normal projective varieties and $D$ is a rational Cartier divisor on $V$, then $$\kappa(V',\pi^*D)=\kappa(V,D).$$ If $\kappa(V,D)=0$, every nonzero space $H^0(V,mD)$ has dimension one.
  - Explanation: Dominant generically finite pullback preserves Iitaka dimension, and a nonzero section space in Kodaira dimension zero is a line.
- **claim-generically-finite-logarithmic-pullback** (theorem, [source](#stmt-p1logcbfalt-log-pullback)): Let $\rho:V\to Y$ be a dominant generically finite morphism of smooth projective varieties. Let $D_Y$ be a reduced snc divisor, and let $B$ be a boundary on $V$ satisfying $B\ge(\rho^*D_Y)_{\mathrm{red}}$. Assume that the boundary supports on the chosen models are snc. Then pullback of rational pluricanonical forms gives $$H^0(Y,m(K_Y+D_Y))\ \longrightarrow\ H^0(V,m(K_V+B))$$ injectively, for divisible $m$.
  - Explanation: A boundary containing the reduced inverse base boundary allows injective pullback of divisible logarithmic pluricanonical systems.
- **claim-projective-relative-iitaka-fibration** (construction, [source](#stmt-p1logcbfalt-iitaka)): Let $p:V\to B$ be a dominant projective morphism, with $V$ smooth projective, and let $D$ be a rational divisor whose restriction to the geometric generic fiber has Iitaka dimension $k\ge0$. After birational modifications there is a relative Iitaka fibration $$V'\xrightarrow{q}U\longrightarrow B$$ in which $q$ has geometrically connected generic fiber, such that $$\dim U-\dim B=k,\qquad \kappa(G,D'\|_G)=0$$ for a very general fiber $G$ of $q$. Here $D'=\mu^*D$, where $\mu:V'\to V$ is the modification. When $D=K_V+B_V$, one may instead take the full log divisor from Lemma <a href="#setup:log-modification" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="setup:log-modification">2</a>.* *If $D'=K_{V'}+B_{V'}$ and the models are log smooth, then $D'\|_G\sim_{\mathbf Q}K_G+B_G$.
  - Explanation: The projective relative Iitaka fibration has the prescribed relative dimension and Kodaira-zero generic Iitaka fibers, with a compatible full-log-divisor replacement.
- **claim-easy-iitaka-upper-addition** (bound, [source](#stmt-p1logcbfalt-easy-addition)): Let $p:V\to B$ be dominant and projective, with $V$ smooth projective and integral very general fiber $G$, and let $D$ be a rational divisor on $V$. Then $$\kappa(V,D)\le \dim B+\kappa(G,D\|_G).$$ If $D$ has a nonzero global section, its restriction to a general fiber is nonzero.
  - Explanation: The total Iitaka image has dimension at most the base dimension plus the fiber contribution. A nonzero global section remains nonzero on a general fiber.

After modifications of the source, adding the reduced exceptional divisor to the strict transform of the boundary, there is a factorization $$X\\xrightarrow{g}W\\xrightarrow{h}Y$$ with smooth compact intermediate space in class $\\mathcal C$, both maps proper with connected fibers, and $$\\dim W_y=k,\\qquad\n \\kappa(G,K_G+\\Delta_G)=0$$ for very general fibers of $h$ and $g$, respectively. The factorization restricts to the log Iitaka fibration on very general $f$-fibers. These properties persist on higher models obtained by the same rule for the source boundary.",
        "source_id": "PM-033.1"
      },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-rankone-comparison",
        "exact_quote": "Let $g:(X,\\Delta)\\to W$ be a fibration with connected fibers from a smooth compact Kähler manifold to a smooth compact manifold in class $\\mathcal C$. Suppose that $\\Delta$ is an SNC boundary with rational coefficients in $[0,1]$ and that $\\kappa(G,K_G+\\Delta_G)=0$ on a very general smooth fiber $G$. After the modifications of [setup:log-modification](#setup:log-modification), [setup:flattening](#setup:flattening), fix a smooth compact reference pair $(X_0,\\Delta_0)$ and a modification $$\\pi:X\\longrightarrow X_0,\\qquad\n \\Delta=\\pi_*^{-1}\\Delta_0+\\operatorname{Exc}(\\pi)_{\\mathrm{red}},$$ such that every prime divisor mapped by $g$ into codimension at least two is $\\pi$-exceptional. Then there are a rational line bundle $M$ and a rational divisor $T$ on $W$ with the following properties.*\n\n1.  *A positive integral multiple of $M$ is the parabolic highest Hodge line of a complex direct summand of a pure, real-polarizable variation with an integral lattice on a dense open subset of $W$. Its parabolic extension is the line to which the adjoint-positivity theorem, [period:adjoint](#period:adjoint), will apply.*\n\n\n2.  *The divisor $T$ is supported on an SNC divisor and satisfies $$\\label{rankone:boundary}\n     B(g,\\Delta)\\leq T\\leq 1.$$ In particular, $T$ is effective. If every prime over a prime $D$ of $W$ has $\\Delta$-coefficient one, then $T_D=1$.*\n\n\n3.  *In sufficiently divisible degrees $q$, multiplication by the relative generating forms gives linear isomorphisms $$\\label{rankone:sections}\n     H^0\\bigl(W,q(K_W+T+M)\\bigr)\n     \\simeq H^0\\bigl(X,q(K_X+\\Delta)\\bigr)\n     \\simeq H^0\\bigl(X_0,q(K_{X_0}+\\Delta_0)\\bigr).$$ In a common divisible grading they preserve products and ratios of sections, and hence the dimensions of the images of their linear systems.*\n\n\n4.  *The same comparison holds relatively. If $h:W\\to Y$ and $f_0:X_0\\to Y$ are holomorphic maps with $f_0\\pi=hg$, then $$\\label{rankone:relative-sections}\n     h_*\\mathcal O_W\\bigl(q(K_W+T+M)\\bigr)\n     \\simeq (f_0)_*\\mathcal O_{X_0}\\bigl(q(K_{X_0}+\\Delta_0)\\bigr)$$ for sufficiently divisible $q$. The comparison of ratios also applies to systems over $Y$ and to their restrictions wherever generic base change holds. The relative identity remains valid after tensoring by a line bundle on $Y$, or a rational line bundle in degrees clearing its denominator.",
        "source_id": "PM-033.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {

Then there are a rational line bundle $M$ and a rational divisor $T$ on $W$ with the following properties.*\n\n1.  *A positive integral multiple of $M$ is the parabolic highest Hodge line of a complex direct summand of a pure, real-polarizable variation with an integral lattice on a dense open subset of $W$.         "anchor": "stmt-application-composition",
        "exact_quote": "Suppose that $X\\xrightarrow{g}W\\xrightarrow{h}Y$ is a diagram of surjective maps between smooth spaces, and $\\Delta,T,C$ are rational boundaries on these spaces. If $$T\\geq B(g,\\Delta),\\qquad C\\leq B(h\\circ g,\\Delta),$$ then $B(h,T)\\geq C$. If, in addition, every prime divisor of $X$ mapped into codimension at least two in $Y$ has coefficient one in $\\Delta$, and $T\\leq1$, then every prime divisor of $W$ mapped into codimension at least two in $Y$ has coefficient one in $T$.",
        "source_id": "PM-033.1"
      },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-period-adjoint",
        "exact_quote": "Let $B$ be a smooth compact connected manifold in class $\\mathcal C$ and $B^\\circ\\subset B$ a dense Zariski open set with SNC complement. Let $\\mathbb V$ be a pure real-polarizable variation of Hodge structures on $B^\\circ$ with an integral ambient lattice. Suppose a complex direct summand of $\\mathbb V_{\\mathbf C}$ has a rank-one highest nonzero Hodge step, with parabolic rational extension $L$. There are a modification $\\tau:B'\\to B$, a surjective morphism $p:B'\\to S$ with connected fibers to a smooth projective variety, and a nef rational line bundle $P$ on $S$ such that $$\\label{period:conclusion}\n \\tau^*L=p^*P,\\qquad K_S+aP\\text{ is big for some }a\\in\\mathbf Q_{>0}.$$ Positive rational rescaling of $L$ is allowed, and the line identity persists under further modifications. If $S$ is a point, $\\tau^*L$ is rationally trivial.",
        "source_id": "PM-033.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-period-adjoint",
          "exact_quote": "Let $B$ be a smooth compact connected manifold in class $\\mathcal C$ and $B^\\circ\\subset B$ a dense Zariski open set with SNC complement. Suppose a complex direct summand of $\\mathbb V_{\\mathbf C}$ has a rank-one highest nonzero Hodge step, with parabolic rational extension $L$. There are a modification $\\tau:B'\\to B$, a surjective morphism $p:B'\\to S$ with connected fibers to a smooth projective variety, and a nef rational line bundle $P$ on $S$ such that $$\\label{period:conclusion}\n \\tau^*L=p^*P,\\qquad K_S+aP\\text{ is big for some }a\\in\\mathbf Q_{>0}.$$ Positive rational rescaling of $L$ is allowed, and the line identity persists under further modifications. If $S$ is a point, $\\tau^*L$ is rationally trivial.",
          "source_id": "PM-033.1"
        }

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-generaltype-addition",
        "exact_quote": "Let $h\\colon V\\to Y$ be a surjective holomorphic map with connected fibers between smooth compact complex manifolds in Fujiki class $\\mathcal C$. Let $T$ and $C$ be effective rational divisors with simple normal crossing support and coefficients in $[0,1]$ on $V$ and $Y$, respectively. Suppose that*\n\n1.  *$K_F+T_F$ is big for a very general smooth fiber $F$ of $h$;*\n\n2.  *$C\\leq B(h,T)$;*\n\n3.  *$T_E=1$ for every prime divisor $E$ of $V$ whose image in $Y$ has codimension at least two.*\n\n*Then $$\\label{generaltype:inequality}\n  \\kappa(V,K_V+T)\\ \\geq\\\n  \\dim F+\\kappa(Y,K_Y+C).$$ As usual, the assertion is automatic if the last term is $-\\infty$.",
        "source_id": "PM-033.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-generaltype-addition",
          "exact_quote": "Let $h\\colon V\\to Y$ be a surjective holomorphic map with connected fibers between smooth compact complex manifolds in Fujiki class $\\mathcal C$. Let $T$ and $C$ be effective rational divisors with simple normal crossing support and coefficients in $[0,1]$ on $V$ and $Y$, respectively.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-addition-principle",
        "exact_quote": "Let $h:W\\to Y$ be a fibration between smooth compact manifolds in class $\\mathcal C$. Let $T,C$ be rational SNC boundaries with $C\\leq B(h,T)$, and suppose every prime of $W$ whose image has codimension at least two in $Y$ has coefficient one in $T$. Let $p:W\\to S$ be a surjective morphism with connected fibers to a smooth projective variety. If $K_W+T+M$ is big on very general $h$-fibers, then $$\\kappa(W,K_W+T+M)\n \\geq\\dim(W/Y)+\\kappa(Y,K_Y+C).$$",
        "source_id": "PM-033.1"

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-period-numerics",
        "exact_quote": "Let $T$ be a smooth compact Kähler manifold, let $E\\subset T$ be a simple normal crossing divisor, and let $A$ be the parabolic extension of the rank-one highest step of a polarizable pure complex variation on $T\\setminus E$ with quasi-unipotent local monodromy. Then $A$ is nef. Its numerical dimension equals the generic rank of the map that remembers this line in a flat trivialization.*\n\n*The corresponding assertion for the Griffiths line, the tensor product of the determinants of the Hodge filtration steps, uses the full period map. In particular, its rational extension is big if the full period map is generically immersive.",

        "exact_quote": "In the setting of [period:adjoint](#period:adjoint), let $G$ be the identity component of the rational Zariski closure of monodromy. Then $G$ is semisimple. Its adjoint local system is a rationally polarized integral variation of weight zero.*\n\n*There is a monodromy-invariant collection of rational simple adjoint factors, each containing a complex factor acting nontrivially on the highest-line orbit, with the following properties.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-generaltype-selection",
        "exact_quote": "Let $Y$ be an irreducible compact complex space. For each positive integer $j$, suppose that $q_j\\colon Q_j\\to Y$ is a proper surjective map from an irreducible compact complex space and that, over a dense analytic open $U_j\\subset Y$, its fibers are irreducible of a fixed dimension $r_j$. Let $$\\phi_j\\colon Q_j\\dashrightarrow Z_j$$ be a meromorphic map with irreducible projective image $Z_j$. Suppose that a dense open part of $q_j^{-1}(U_j)$, called the genuine frame locus, meets every fiber densely and that $\\phi_j$ is holomorphic there. Let $G_j\\subset Z_j$ be a countable union of algebraically constructible subsets.*\n\n*Let $\\Lambda_j\\subset U_j$ be sets whose union contains a very general subset of $Y$, such that for $y\\in\\Lambda_j$ all genuine frames above $y$ map into $G_j$. Then some $\\Lambda_j$ is not contained in a countable union of proper closed analytic subsets of $Y$, and for that index one of the constructible subsets making up $G_j$ is dense in $Z_j$. In particular $G_j$ contains a nonempty algebraic open subset of $Z_j$.",
        "source_id": "PM-033.1"
      },

        "exact_quote": "Let $h\\colon V\\to Y$ be a surjective holomorphic map with connected fibers, where $V$ is a smooth compact Kähler manifold and $Y$ is a smooth compact manifold in class $\\mathcal C$. Let $A$ be an effective rational SNC divisor on $V$, horizontal over $Y$, with coefficients strictly less than one. Suppose $K_F+A_F$ is big on very general fibers $F$ of positive dimension. There are*\n\n1.  *a proper generically finite map $\\tau\\colon Y'\\to Y$, with $Y'$ smooth, compact, and in class $\\mathcal C$;*\n\n2.  *a surjective holomorphic map $\\rho\\colon Y'\\to R$, with $R$ smooth and projective;*\n\n3.  *a projective stable family $\\pi\\colon(\\mathcal V,\\mathcal A)\\to R$ of maximal variation, with klt general fiber;*\n\n*such that the main component of $V\\times_Y Y'$ is bimeromorphic over $Y'$ to $\\mathcal V\\times_RY'$.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-generaltype-relative-system",
        "exact_quote": "Under the hypotheses of [generaltype:stable-comparison](#generaltype:stable-comparison), there is a positive integer $b_0$ and a finite-dimensional space $$\\mathcal S\\subset\n  H^0_{\\mathrm{mer}}\\bigl(V,\\mathcal O_V(b_0K_{V/Y})\\bigr)$$ whose associated meromorphic map has rank $\\dim F$ on general $h$-fibers, with the following properties:*\n\n1.  *every member has horizontal pole bound $b_0A$;*\n\n2.  *every member satisfies the relative logarithmic bound at primes over base divisors;*\n\n3.  *the same bound may be tested after any modification of the base, at a divisor exposed on that modification.*\n\n*The same assertions hold for the systems of products of members of $\\mathcal S$.",

        "exact_quote": "Let $f:X\\to Y$ be a surjective morphism with connected fibers between smooth connected projective complex varieties. Then $$\\label{spec:log-inequality}\n \\kappa(X,K_X+D_X)\n \\geq\\kappa(F,K_F+D_F)+\\kappa(Y,K_Y+D_Y).$$ The same statement holds for compact manifolds in Fujiki class $\\mathcal C$ and holomorphic fibrations between them.",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-spec-ordinary",
        "exact_quote": "Let $k$ be an algebraically closed field of characteristic zero, and let $f:X\\to Y$ be a surjective morphism with connected fibers between smooth connected projective $k$-varieties. If $F$ is its geometric generic fiber, then $$\\label{spec:ordinary-inequality}\n \\kappa(X)\\geq\\kappa(F)+\\kappa(Y).$$ More generally, the logarithmic inequality of Corollary <a href=\"#spec:logarithmic\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"spec:logarithmic\">31</a>, with the same boundary hypotheses, holds over $k$, with the geometric generic fiber in place of a very general complex fiber.",
        "source_id": "PM-033.1"
      },

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-spec-core-dimension",
        "exact_quote": "Let $(X,\\Delta)$ be a smooth compact orbifold pair in Fujiki class $\\mathcal C$, with rational simple normal crossing boundary coefficients in $[0,1]$. Let $C=C(X,\\Delta)$ be its core base and $(G,\\Delta_G)$ a very general orbifold fiber. Then $$\\label{spec:core-equality}\n \\kappa(X,K_X+\\Delta)\n   =\\kappa(G,K_G+\\Delta_G)+\\dim C.$$ If $\\kappa(X,K_X+\\Delta)\\geq0$, then $$0\\leq\\dim C\\leq\\kappa(X,K_X+\\Delta),$$ and equality in the second inequality holds precisely when $\\kappa(G,K_G+\\Delta_G)=0$. In particular, $\\kappa(X,K_X+\\Delta)=0$ implies that $(X,\\Delta)$ is special.",
        "source_id": "PM-033.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-spec-core-dimension",
          "exact_quote": "Let $(X,\\Delta)$ be a smooth compact orbifold pair in Fujiki class $\\mathcal C$, with rational simple normal crossing boundary coefficients in $[0,1]$. In particular, $\\kappa(X,K_X+\\Delta)=0$ implies that $(X,\\Delta)$ is special.",
          "source_id": "PM-033.1"

      "explanation": "The core gives equality between the total log Kodaira dimension and the fiber contribution plus core dimension. Log Kodaira dimension zero implies specialness; the converse is not asserted.",

      "id": "claim-core-kodaira-additivity",
      "kind": "theorem",
      "quantifiers": [],
      "statement_anchor": "stmt-spec-core-dimension"

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-spec-core-tower",
        "exact_quote": "Let $(X,\\Delta)$ be as in Corollary <a href=\"#spec:core-dimension\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"spec:core-dimension\">33</a>, and set $n=\\dim X$. Starting from $(X,\\Delta)$, successively take the $\\kappa$-rational quotient $r$ and the Moishezon–Iitaka fibration $M$ of its stable orbifold base. At each iteration choose such compatible representatives and equip the new base with the stable inf-multiplicity orbifold structure of the composite. The composite after at most $n$ iterations is the core, up to bimeromorphic equivalence of fibrations: $$c_{(X,\\Delta)}=(M\\circ r)^n.$$ The positive-dimensional very general stable orbifold fibers of the $r$-steps have $\\kappa_+=-\\infty$, and those of the $M$-steps have Kodaira dimension zero. In particular, for a special pair this tower ends at a point.",
        "source_id": "PM-033.1"
      },

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-reduced-cyclic-top",
        "exact_quote": "Let $(J,D)$ be a smooth geometrically integral projective pair over a field $K$ of characteristic zero. Assume that $D$ is reduced and has simple normal crossings and that $\\kappa(J_{\\overline K},K_{J_{\\overline K}}+D_{\\overline K})=0$. Choose the least positive integer $p$ for which $H^0(J,p(K_J+D))\\ne0$, a nonzero section $\\omega$ in that space, and put $$E=\\operatorname{div}(\\omega)+pD,\n \\qquad \\Delta=D-\\frac1pE.$$ Write $\\omega=b\\eta^{\\otimes p}$ in a rational canonical frame, and let $\\tau:N\\to J$ be the normalization in $K(J)[t]/(t^p-b)$. Let $\\rho:\\widetilde N\\to N$ be a log resolution, and set $$P=\\operatorname{Supp}(\\Delta^{=1}),\\qquad\n D_{\\widetilde N}=\\bigl((\\tau\\circ\\rho)^{-1}P\\bigr)_{\\mathrm{red}}.$$ Then $N$ is geometrically integral, the rational top form $\\Omega=t\\tau^*\\eta$ extends logarithmically to $(\\widetilde N,D_{\\widetilde N})$, and $$H^0\\bigl(\\widetilde N,m(K_{\\widetilde N}+D_{\\widetilde N})\\bigr)\n       =K\\,\\Omega^m\\qquad(m\\ge1).$$",
        "source_id": "PM-033.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-reduced-cyclic-top",
          "exact_quote": "Let $(J,D)$ be a smooth geometrically integral projective pair over a field $K$ of characteristic zero. Choose the least positive integer $p$ for which $H^0(J,p(K_J+D))\\ne0$, a nonzero section $\\omega$ in that space, and put $$E=\\operatorname{div}(\\omega)+pD,\n \\qquad \\Delta=D-\\frac1pE.$$ Write $\\omega=b\\eta^{\\otimes p}$ in a rational canonical frame, and let $\\tau:N\\to J$ be the normalization in $K(J)[t]/(t^p-b)$. Let $\\rho:\\widetilde N\\to N$ be a log resolution, and set $$P=\\operatorname{Supp}(\\Delta^{=1}),\\qquad\n D_{\\widetilde N}=\\bigl((\\tau\\circ\\rho)^{-1}P\\bigr)_{\\mathrm{red}}.$$ Then $N$ is geometrically integral, the rational top form $\\Omega=t\\tau^*\\eta$ extends logarithmically to $(\\widetilde N,D_{\\widetilde N})$, and $$H^0\\bigl(\\widetilde N,m(K_{\\widetilde N}+D_{\\widetilde N})\\bigr)\n       =K\\,\\Omega^m\\qquad(m\\ge1).$$",
          "source_id": "PM-033.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Let $(J,D)$ be a smooth geometrically integral projective pair over a field $K$ of characteristic zero. Choose the least positive integer $p$ for which $H^0(J,p(K_J+D))\\ne0$, a nonzero section $\\omega$ in that space, and put $$E=\\operatorname{div}(\\omega)+pD,\n \\qquad \\Delta=D-\\frac1pE.$$ Write $\\omega=b\\eta^{\\otimes p}$ in a rational canonical frame, and let $\\tau:N\\to J$ be the normalization in $K(J)[t]/(t^p-b)$.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-cor-reduced-integral-moduli",
        "exact_quote": "Let $x:X\\to W$ be a projective fibration between smooth complex projective varieties, with geometrically integral generic fiber $J$. Let $D_X$ be a reduced SNC boundary whose restriction $D_J$ satisfies the hypotheses of Lemma <a href=\"#lem:reduced-cyclic-top\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:reduced-cyclic-top\">35</a>. Let $M_W$ be the rational Hodge divisor of the rank-one reduction, with its rational frame and parabolic orders as in Equation <a href=\"#rankone:order\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"rankone:order\">[rankone:order]</a>. On sufficiently high smooth models, there is a polarizable integral pure variation of Hodge structures on a dense smooth open $W^\\circ\\subset W$ whose top nonzero Hodge filtration piece is a line, and $$M_W\\sim_{\\mathbf Q}\n \\text{the rational canonical extension of that line}.$$ In the canonical-bundle-formula normalization, this is the moduli divisor of the auxiliary generic subpair $\\Delta_J=D_J-E_J/p$, which may have negative coefficients.",
        "source_id": "PM-033.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-cor-reduced-integral-moduli",
          "exact_quote": "Let $x:X\\to W$ be a projective fibration between smooth complex projective varieties, with geometrically integral generic fiber $J$. Let $M_W$ be the rational Hodge divisor of the rank-one reduction, with its rational frame and parabolic orders as in Equation <a href=\"#rankone:order\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"rankone:order\">[rankone:order]</a>. On sufficiently high smooth models, there is a polarizable integral pure variation of Hodge structures on a dense smooth open $W^\\circ\\subset W$ whose top nonzero Hodge filtration piece is a line, and $$M_W\\sim_{\\mathbf Q}\n \\text{the rational canonical extension of that line}.$$ In the canonical-bundle-formula normalization, this is the moduli divisor of the auxiliary generic subpair $\\Delta_J=D_J-E_J/p$, which may have negative coefficients.",
          "source_id": "PM-033.1"
        }

      "exact_statement": "Let $x:X\\to W$ be a projective fibration between smooth complex projective varieties, with geometrically integral generic fiber $J$. Let $M_W$ be the rational Hodge divisor of the rank-one reduction, with its rational frame and parabolic orders as in Equation <a href=\"#rankone:order\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"rankone:order\">[rankone:order]</a>. Let $M_W$ be the rational Hodge divisor of the rank-one reduction, with its rational frame and parabolic orders as in Equation <a href=\"#rankone:order\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"rankone:order\">[rankone:order]</a>.",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-rankone-normalization",
        "exact_quote": "Let $x:X'\\to W$ be a projective fibration on smooth models, let $D'$ be an effective reduced SNC divisor, and suppose that its geometric generic fiber $(J,D_J)$ has logarithmic Kodaira dimension zero. Fix a nonzero relative rational $p$-canonical form $s$ generating $H^0(J,p(K_J+D_J))$. At a prime $P$ of $W$ set $$a_E=\\operatorname{ord}_E(x^*P),\\qquad\n r_E=p^{-1}\\operatorname{ord}^{pK_{X'/W}}_E(s),\\qquad\n d_E=\\operatorname{coeff}_E(D'),\\qquad\n t_P=\\min_{E\\mapsto P}\\frac{r_E+d_E}{a_E}.$$ Here the minimum is over actual components on the chosen source model. Choose that model so that the generator divisor, boundary and inverse image of $P$ have SNC support over the generic point of $P$; later source modifications carry strict boundary plus reduced exceptional boundary. The coefficients in the rank-one comparison satisfy $$\\alpha_P=t_P,\\qquad\n \\beta_P=\\inf_{E\\mapsto P}\\frac{1+r_E}{a_E}-1,\\qquad\n T_P=1-c_P,\n \\quad\n c_P:=\\inf_{E\\mapsto P}\\frac{1+r_E}{a_E}-t_P.$$ The infima allow divisorial valuations on higher smooth source models. Equivalently, $c_P$ is the log canonical threshold of $x^*P$ for the normalized auxiliary subpair $$\\Delta^{\\mathrm{aux}}\n =-p^{-1}\\operatorname{div}_{K_{X'/W}}(s)+x^*\\sum_Qt_QQ.$$ If $T_P=0$, an actual component attaining $t_P$ has $a_E=1$, $d_E=0$, and attains the threshold infimum as well.",
        "source_id": "PM-033.1"
      },

Choose that model so that the generator divisor, boundary and inverse image of $P$ have SNC support over the generic point of $P$; later source modifications carry strict boundary plus reduced exceptional boundary. The coefficients in the rank-one comparison satisfy $$\\alpha_P=t_P,\\qquad\n \\beta_P=\\inf_{E\\mapsto P}\\frac{1+r_E}{a_E}-1,\\qquad\n T_P=1-c_P,\n \\quad\n c_P:=\\inf_{E\\mapsto P}\\frac{1+r_E}{a_E}-t_P.$$ The infima allow divisorial valuations on higher smooth source models. Equivalently, $c_P$ is the log canonical threshold of $x^*P$ for the normalized auxiliary subpair $$\\Delta^{\\mathrm{aux}}\n =-p^{-1}\\operatorname{div}_{K_{X'/W}}(s)+x^*\\sum_Qt_QQ.$$ If $T_P=0$, an actual component attaining $t_P$ has $a_E=1$, $d_E=0$, and attains the threshold infimum as well.",

<!-- PM:END pm.claims.v2 -->

<a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **Rational SNC boundary** ([source](#pm-h-3)): A rational SNC boundary on a smooth manifold is a finite sum $\Delta=\sum_i\delta_i\Delta_i$, with $\delta_i\in\mathbf Q\cap[0,1]$ and simple normal crossing support.
- **Source boundary multiplicity** ([source](#pm-h-3)): Set $$m_\Delta(E)=\frac1{1-\Delta_E}, \qquad \frac1{0}=\infty,$$ where $\Delta_E=0$ off the boundary.
- **Inf-multiplicity orbifold base** ([source](#pm-h-3)): If the base of $f:(X,\Delta)\to Y$ is smooth, define $$\label{setup:inf-boundary} \begin{gathered} m(f,\Delta;D)=\min_{E\mapsto D} \bigl\{\operatorname{ord}_E(f^*D)m_\Delta(E)\bigr\}, \qquad B(f,\Delta)=\sum_D\left(1-\frac1{m(f,\Delta;D)}\right)D. \end{gathered}$$
- **Invariant orbifold base dimension** ([source](#setup:orbifold-morphism)): Define $$\label{setup:invariant-base} \kappa(f\mid\Delta)= \inf_{f':(X',\Delta')\to Y'\sim f} \kappa\bigl(Y',K_{Y'}+B(f',\Delta')\bigr).$$
- **Iitaka dimension of a rational line bundle** ([source](#pm-h-3)): For a rational line bundle $L$, $\kappa(Z,L)$ is the maximal image dimension of its complete linear systems in sufficiently divisible positive degrees, and is $-\infty$ if all these systems are empty.
- **Fujiki class C** ([source](#pm-h-3)): Fujiki class $\mathcal C$ consists of compact complex spaces bimeromorphic to compact Kähler spaces.
- **Neat fibration** ([source](#setup:neat-formula)): A smooth-base fibration $g:(Z,\Gamma)\to T$ is *neat* if there is a proper bimeromorphic orbifold morphism $w:(Z,\Gamma)\to(Z_0,\Gamma_0)$ to a smooth pair, with $w_*\Gamma=\Gamma_0$, such that every prime divisor sent by $g$ into codimension at least two is also sent by $w$ into codimension at least two.
- **Rational-line effectivity** ([source](#setup:section)): A rational line is effective if a positive integral multiple has a nonzero section.
- **Highest Hodge step** ([source](#rankone:section)): The *highest step* of a summand is its highest nonzero filtration piece.
- **Parabolic extension** ([source](#rankone:section)): The *parabolic extension* of such a line is obtained by local power substitutions making boundary monodromy unipotent, extension of the unipotent Hodge filtration, and descent with the resulting rational weights.
- **Extreme Hodge line** ([source](#period:section)): Here an *extreme Hodge line* means the rank-one highest nonzero filtration step of a complex Hodge direct summand.
- **Line map and full period map** ([source](#period:section)): In a flat trivialization the *line map* records only the chosen highest line; the *period map* records all Hodge filtration steps.
- **Numerical dimension of a nef rational line** ([source](#pm-h-22)): For a nef rational line bundle $A$ on a compact Kähler $n$-fold, we use $$\nu(A)=\max\{k:c_1(A)^k\cdot[\omega]^{n-k}>0\},$$ where $\omega$ is any Kähler form.
- **Stable pair** ([source](#pm-h-28)): A stable pair has semi-log-canonical singularities and an ample rational log canonical divisor.
- **Stable family** ([source](#pm-h-28)): A stable family over a normal base is flat, proper and surjective with connected stable fibers; its boundary support avoids the generic points and the codimension-one singular points of every fiber.
- **Maximal variation** ([source](#pm-h-28)): A stable family has *maximal variation* when its classifying map to the moduli space of stable pairs is generically finite onto its image.
- **Relative logarithmic bound** ([source](#pm-h-29)): We say that $\sigma$ has the relative logarithmic bound at this prime if $$\label{generaltype:relative-log-bound} \mathop{\mathrm{ord}}_E\bigl(\sigma\wedge h^*\omega_D^{\,b}\bigr)\geq-b.$$
- **Special orbifold pair** ([source](#spec:core)): A smooth compact orbifold pair $(X,\Delta)$ in class $\mathcal C$ is *special* if it has no meromorphic fibration onto a positive-dimensional base of orbifold general type; here general type means that the invariant base dimension equals the dimension of the base.
- **Strictly neat representative** ([source](#spec:core)): A neat representative $g:(Z,\Gamma)\to T$ in the sense of Section <a href="#setup:section" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="setup:section">2</a> is *strictly neat* if its inf-multiplicity boundary on the smooth base is SNC.
- **High representative** ([source](#spec:core)): It is *high* if $g$ is an orbifold morphism to this base: for every base prime $D$ and source prime $E$ with $a_E=\mathop{\mathrm{ord}}_E(g^*D)>0$, including primes contracted by $g$, $$a_E m_\Gamma(E)\geq m_{B(g,\Gamma)}(D).$$
- **Negative kappa-plus condition** ([source](#spec:core-tower-section)): For a smooth orbifold pair $(Z,\Theta)$, the notation $\kappa_+(Z,\Theta)=-\infty$ means that every meromorphic fibration onto a positive-dimensional base has invariant orbifold Kodaira dimension $-\infty$; the condition is vacuous for a point.
- **Kappa-rational quotient** ([source](#spec:core-tower-section)): The *$\kappa$-rational quotient* is a meromorphic fibration $r:(Z,\Theta)\dashrightarrow R$ with $\kappa(r\mid\Theta)\geq0$ whose very general stable orbifold fiber has $\kappa_+=-\infty$ (Campana 2011, Definition 5.23 and Corollary 6.14) [[CampanaOrbifolds](#ref-CampanaOrbifolds)].

| id | quantity | relation | value | regime |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| q-orbifold-subadditivity-bound | \kappa(X,K_X+\Delta) | ≥ | $\kappa(F,K_F+\Delta_F)+\kappa(f\mid\Delta)$ | The fiber is very general and smooth; the base term is the invariant over allowed models. |
| q-relative-iitaka-image-dimension | \dim W_y | = | $k$ | Very general fibers of the intermediate map to the original base. |
| q-relative-iitaka-fiber-kodaira | \kappa(G,K_G+\Delta_G) | = | $0$ | Very general smooth Iitaka fibers with the induced boundary. |
| q-rank-one-boundary-lower-bound | T | ≥ | $B(g,\Delta)$ | Coefficientwise comparison of rational divisors on the intermediate base. |
| q-rank-one-boundary-upper-bound | T | ≤ | $1$ | Each coefficient of the resulting intermediate boundary. |
| q-parabolic-frame-order | \beta_D | = | $\min_{g(E)=D}\frac{l_E+1-a_E}{a_E}$ | The minimum includes exceptional primes introduced by resolution. |
| q-boundary-correction-order-difference | T_D | = | $\alpha_D-\beta_D$ | A fixed base prime and the local rational generator. |
| q-log-general-type-addition | \kappa(V,K_V+T) | ≥ | $\dim F+\kappa(Y,K_Y+C)$ | The fiber adjoint is big and the base contribution is logarithmic. |
| q-adjoint-addition | \kappa(W,K_W+T+M) | ≥ | $\dim(W/Y)+\kappa(Y,K_Y+C)$ | The projective base has the required big adjoint twist. |
| q-marked-boundary-numerical-drop | \nu(P\|_{D_i}) | < | $\nu(P)$ | Every marked boundary component, including finite nonidentity local monodromy. |
| q-logarithmic-subadditivity | \kappa(X,K_X+D_X) | ≥ | $\kappa(F,K_F+D_F)+\kappa(Y,K_Y+D_Y)$ | Reduced SNC boundaries satisfying the pullback-support inclusion. |
| q-ordinary-characteristic-zero-bound | \kappa(X) | ≥ | $\kappa(F)+\kappa(Y)$ | A surjective connected-fiber morphism and its geometric generic fiber. |
| q-core-additivity | \kappa(X,K_X+\Delta) | = | $\kappa(G,K_G+\Delta_G)+\dim C$ | The very general stable orbifold core fiber and the core base. |
| q-core-tower-iteration-bound | core tower iterations | ≤ | $n$ | The source dimension bounds nontrivial iterations; identity steps give the displayed composite. |
| q-cyclic-root-divisorial-order | \operatorname{ord}_Q\Omega | = | $\frac{ea}{p}-ed+e-1$ | A covering prime, its ramification index and the corresponding boundary and zero-divisor coefficients. |
| q-normalized-boundary-threshold | T_P | = | $1-c_P$ | The threshold uses all higher divisorial valuations of the normalized auxiliary subpair. |
| q-easy-addition-upper-bound | \kappa(V,D) | ≤ | $\dim B+\kappa(G,D\|_G)$ | A rational divisor and an integral very general fiber. |

          "exact_quote": "Let $g:(X,\\Delta)\\to W$ be a fibration with connected fibers from a smooth compact Kähler manifold to a smooth compact manifold in class $\\mathcal C$. After the modifications of [setup:log-modification](#setup:log-modification), [setup:flattening](#setup:flattening), fix a smooth compact reference pair $(X_0,\\Delta_0)$ and a modification $$\\pi:X\\longrightarrow X_0,\\qquad\n \\Delta=\\pi_*^{-1}\\Delta_0+\\operatorname{Exc}(\\pi)_{\\mathrm{red}},$$ such that every prime divisor mapped by $g$ into codimension at least two is $\\pi$-exceptional. Then there are a rational line bundle $M$ and a rational divisor $T$ on $W$ with the following properties.*\n\n1.  *A positive integral multiple of $M$ is the parabolic highest Hodge line of a complex direct summand of a pure, real-polarizable variation with an integral lattice on a dense open subset of $W$. Its parabolic extension is the line to which the adjoint-positivity theorem, [period:adjoint](#period:adjoint), will apply.*\n\n\n2.  *The divisor $T$ is supported on an SNC divisor and satisfies $$\\label{rankone:boundary}\n     B(g,\\Delta)\\leq T\\leq 1.$$ In particular, $T$ is effective. If every prime over a prime $D$ of $W$ has $\\Delta$-coefficient one, then $T_D=1$.*\n\n\n3.  *In sufficiently divisible degrees $q$, multiplication by the relative generating forms gives linear isomorphisms $$\\label{rankone:sections}\n     H^0\\bigl(W,q(K_W+T+M)\\bigr)\n     \\simeq H^0\\bigl(X,q(K_X+\\Delta)\\bigr)\n     \\simeq H^0\\bigl(X_0,q(K_{X_0}+\\Delta_0)\\bigr).$$ In a common divisible grading they preserve products and ratios of sections, and hence the dimensions of the images of their linear systems.*\n\n\n4.  *The same comparison holds relatively. If $h:W\\to Y$ and $f_0:X_0\\to Y$ are holomorphic maps with $f_0\\pi=hg$, then $$\\label{rankone:relative-sections}\n     h_*\\mathcal O_W\\bigl(q(K_W+T+M)\\bigr)\n     \\simeq (f_0)_*\\mathcal O_{X_0}\\bigl(q(K_{X_0}+\\Delta_0)\\bigr)$$ for sufficiently divisible $q$. The comparison of ratios also applies to systems over $Y$ and to their restrictions wherever generic base change holds. The relative identity remains valid after tensoring by a line bundle on $Y$, or a rational line bundle in degrees clearing its denominator.",
          "source_id": "PM-033.1"
        }
      ],

          "exact_quote": "Let $h\\colon V\\to Y$ be a surjective holomorphic map with connected fibers between smooth compact complex manifolds in Fujiki class $\\mathcal C$.           "exact_quote": "Let $(X,\\Delta)$ be a smooth compact orbifold pair in Fujiki class $\\mathcal C$, with rational simple normal crossing boundary coefficients in $[0,1]$. Let $C=C(X,\\Delta)$ be its core base and $(G,\\Delta_G)$ a very general orbifold fiber.           "anchor": "stmt-spec-core-tower",
          "exact_quote": "Let $(X,\\Delta)$ be as in Corollary <a href=\"#spec:core-dimension\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"spec:core-dimension\">33</a>, and set $n=\\dim X$. Starting from $(X,\\Delta)$, successively take the $\\kappa$-rational quotient $r$ and the Moishezon–Iitaka fibration $M$ of its stable orbifold base. At each iteration choose such compatible representatives and equip the new base with the stable inf-multiplicity orbifold structure of the composite. The composite after at most $n$ iterations is the core, up to bimeromorphic equivalence of fibrations: $$c_{(X,\\Delta)}=(M\\circ r)^n.$$ The positive-dimensional very general stable orbifold fibers of the $r$-steps have $\\kappa_+=-\\infty$, and those of the $M$-steps have Kodaira dimension zero. In particular, for a special pair this tower ends at a point.",
          "source_id": "PM-033.1"
        }
