<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **step-log-modification** [claim](#stmt-setup-log-modification) / [proof](#proof-stmt-setup-log-modification): Logarithmic Jacobian discrepancy and exceptional-function descent. -> Effective exceptional discrepancy and equality of divisible log section rings.
- **step-exceptional-descent** [claim](#stmt-setup-exceptional-descent) / [proof](#proof-stmt-setup-exceptional-descent): Divisorial regularity followed by Hartogs extension. -> A section on the original smooth pair, including pulled-back twists.
- **step-modification-compatibility** [claim](#stmt-setup-modification-compatibility) / [proof](#proof-stmt-setup-modification-compatibility): Restriction to very general fibers and comparison of old and exceptional multiplicities. -> Fiber invariance, compositional compatibility and fixed-base boundary invariance.
- **step-flatten-reference** [claim](#stmt-setup-flattening) / [proof](#proof-stmt-setup-flattening): Analytic flattening before source resolution and a fiber-dimension estimate. -> A neat model whose contracted divisors are exceptional over the original reference.
- **step-simultaneous-control** [claim](#stmt-setup-simultaneous) / [proof](#proof-stmt-setup-simultaneous): Track strict transforms and reduced exceptional divisors over the fixed original base. -> Simultaneous exceptional-reference control and preservation of the original inf-multiplicity base.
- **step-relative-iitaka** [claim](#stmt-application-relative-iitaka) / [proof](#proof-stmt-application-relative-iitaka): Coherent direct images, generic base change, stabilized relative systems and Stein factorization. -> A relative Iitaka factorization with log Kodaira-zero fibers.
- **step-root-eigenspace** [claim](#stmt-rankone-eigenspace) / [proof](#proof-stmt-rankone-eigenspace): Logarithmic Hodge theory and descent of ratios of equal-character forms. -> The tautological character part of the highest Hodge step is a line.
- **step-tensor-descent** [claim](#stmt-rankone-descent) / [proof](#proof-stmt-rankone-descent): Tensor powers and deck invariants kill root-choice and cocycle ambiguity. -> A global integral pure variation containing a positive tensor power of the local line.
- **step-canonical-extension** [claim](#stmt-rankone-extension) / [proof](#proof-stmt-rankone-extension): Unipotentization, locally free double gradeds and strictness of the weight filtration. -> An isomorphism of canonical extensions and of parabolic rational lines.
- **step-rank-one-comparison** [claim](#stmt-rankone-comparison) / [proof](#proof-stmt-rankone-collision): Compute parabolic and pluriform orders at every prepared prime, then descend untested exceptional poles. -> The effective bounded intermediate boundary and exact absolute and relative section comparisons.
- **step-compose-multiplicities** [claim](#stmt-application-composition) / [proof](#proof-stmt-application-composition): Multiply pullback orders and take the prescribed inf-multiplicity minima. -> Boundary domination and coefficient-one control for the intermediate fibration.
- **step-curvature-numerics** [claim](#stmt-period-numerics) / [proof](#proof-stmt-period-numerics): Unipotent reduction, Hodge curvature, current regularization and full-mass intersection identities. -> Nefness and equality of numerical dimension with line-map rank, with the Griffiths-line analogue.
- **step-boundary-restriction** [claim](#stmt-period-boundary) / [proof](#proof-stmt-period-boundary): Limiting mixed filtrations, monodromy grading and multivariable extension. -> The restricted line equals the extended limiting graded highest line.
- **step-adjoint-reduction** [claim](#stmt-period-adjoint-reduction) / [proof](#proof-stmt-period-adjoint-reduction): Fixed-part and finite-determinant arguments, rational adjoint factors and highest-weight tensor generation. -> Discrete integral adjoint monodromy and recovery of a power of the line through a tensor construction.
- **step-period-stabilizer** [claim](#stmt-period-stabilizer) / [proof](#proof-stmt-period-stabilizer): A polarized integral fixed-type span has finite monodromy; the adjoint centralizer is trivial. -> The automorphism is the identity.
- **step-projective-quotient** [claim](#stmt-period-quotient) / [proof](#proof-stmt-period-quotient): Proper period extension, connected-fiber compactification, projectivity from the Griffiths line and finite-level descent. -> A projective period quotient and an exact pullback identity for the parabolic nef line.
- **step-marked-boundary-rankloss** [claim](#stmt-period-rankloss) / [proof](#proof-stmt-period-rankloss): Monodromy normality and Ax–Schanuel decompose line fibers; limiting fixed loci and generic stabilizers force boundary rank loss. -> Strict numerical dimension loss on every marked component, including finite monodromy.
- **step-boundary-subtraction** [claim](#stmt-period-subtract) / [proof](#proof-stmt-period-subtract): Divisor exact sequences and asymptotic Riemann–Roch compare ambient section growth with boundary losses. -> Bigness after boundary removal and a sufficiently large rational nef twist.
- **step-adjoint-positivity** [claim](#stmt-period-adjoint) / [proof](#proof-stmt-period-subtract-2): Logarithmic general type on the immersive period quotient followed by boundary subtraction. -> A big canonical divisor after a positive rational multiple of the descended line.
- **step-good-degree-selection** [claim](#stmt-generaltype-selection) / [proof](#proof-stmt-generaltype-selection): Baire category, proper fiber-dimension loci and density of constructible subsets. -> An index whose good parameter image contains a nonempty algebraic open.
- **step-stable-comparison** [claim](#stmt-generaltype-stable-comparison) / [proof](#proof-stmt-generaltype-stable-comparison): Relative images and frame spaces, stable moduli, a finite parameter cover and a compactified isomorphism cover. -> A generically finite comparison with a projective stable family of maximal variation.
- **step-stable-valuation-bounds** [claim](#stmt-generaltype-stable-orders) / [proof](#proof-stmt-generaltype-stable-orders): Canonical-model discrepancy horizontally and inversion of adjunction on transverse stable disk families vertically. -> Horizontal and vertical logarithmic bounds on all smooth higher base models.
- **step-relative-section-system** [claim](#stmt-generaltype-relative-system) / [proof](#proof-stmt-generaltype-relative-system): Stable-family positivity, finite invariant algebras and exact ramified order descent. -> A descended finite-dimensional relative system of full fiber rank with bounds preserved under products.
- **step-expose-valuation** [claim](#stmt-generaltype-expose-valuation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-generaltype-expose-valuation): Analytic flattening and preservation of the generic point of an original source divisor. -> A modified base prime dominated by its strict transform.
- **step-general-type-addition** [claim](#stmt-generaltype-addition) / [proof](#proof-stmt-generaltype-expose-valuation-2): Lower horizontal coefficients, construct bounded relative forms, and multiply by base sections while preserving fiber and base ratios. -> The log-general-type-fiber subadditivity lower bound.
- **step-big-twist-effectivity** [claim](#stmt-application-weak-effectivity) / [proof](#proof-stmt-application-weak-effectivity): Weak positivity, multiplication of relative sections, big base twists and exceptional descent. -> A nonzero section of a positive relative adjoint multiple with that base twist.
- **step-adjoint-addition** [claim](#stmt-addition-principle) / [proof](#proof-stmt-addition-principle): General-type addition after an ample perturbation and exact cancellation by powers of a fixed negatively twisted section. -> The adjoint lower bound with coefficient one on the moduli line.
- **step-main-subadditivity** [claim](#stmt-main-orbifold) / [proof](#proof-stmt-generaltype-expose-valuation-3): Fix the original base, reduce relatively, compare section rings, apply adjoint addition and descend to the original source. -> Orbifold Iitaka subadditivity.
- **step-log-base-comparison** [claim](#stmt-spec-base-comparison) / [proof](#proof-stmt-spec-base-comparison): Choose a neat model and pull back logarithmic pluriforms with unchanged ratios. -> The invariant base dimension dominates the prescribed logarithmic base dimension.
- **step-logarithmic-specialization** [claim](#stmt-spec-logarithmic) / [proof](#proof-stmt-spec-logarithmic): Apply orbifold subadditivity and replace its invariant base term by the logarithmic lower bound. -> Logarithmic subadditivity.
- **step-characteristic-zero-specialization** [claim](#stmt-spec-ordinary) / [proof](#proof-stmt-spec-ordinary): Use empty boundaries, generic base change and invariance under ground-field extension after descent to a finitely generated subfield. -> Ordinary and logarithmic subadditivity over algebraically closed characteristic-zero fields.
- **step-core-dimension** [claim](#stmt-spec-core-dimension) / [proof](#proof-stmt-spec-core-dimension): Combine the core's general-type base with subadditivity and the elementary image-dimension upper bound. -> Exact core additivity, the core-dimension bound and the Kodaira-zero specialness consequence.
- **step-core-tower** [claim](#stmt-spec-core-tower) / [proof](#proof-stmt-spec-core-tower): Apply Campana's quotient existence and decomposition results with compatible strictly neat and high representatives. -> The alternating quotient and Iitaka tower stabilizes at the core within the dimension bound.
- **step-projective-section-test** [claim](#stmt-p1logcbfalt-section-test) / [proof](#proof-stmt-p1logcbfalt-section-test): Intersect codimension-one lattices of a reflexive sheaf and use finite Galois descent. -> Divisorial section membership and the invariant-section identity for finite Galois covers.
- **step-finite-pullback** [claim](#stmt-p1logcbfalt-finite) / [proof](#proof-stmt-p1logcbfalt-finite): Stein factorization and characteristic-polynomial integrality of graded sections. -> Iitaka-dimension invariance and one-dimensional nonzero systems in Kodaira dimension zero.
- **step-reduced-root-cover** [claim](#stmt-lem-reduced-cyclic-top) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-reduced-cyclic-top): Minimal-index irreducibility, ramification discrepancy scaling and domination by the pulled-back fixed zero divisor. -> A geometrically integral minimal root cover with its entire logarithmic pluriform space generated by the root form.
- **step-integral-moduli-line** [claim](#stmt-cor-reduced-integral-moduli) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-reduced-integral-moduli): Spread the generic minimal cover, isolate the pure weight grade and match exact parabolic orders and discrepancy-sheaf rank. -> An integral pure realization with exact rational moduli normalization.
- **step-threshold-normalization** [claim](#stmt-lem-rankone-normalization) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-rankone-normalization): Identify the actual relative orders, compare SNC minima with all valuations and analyze equality at an actual minimizing component. -> The threshold formula for the boundary and the multiplicity-one consequence of a vanishing coefficient.
- **step-permitted-normalized-models** [claim](#stmt-lem-normalized-models) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-normalized-models): Effective exceptional discrepancy and the projection formula with pulled-back moduli. -> Preserved divisible section spaces and unchanged old divisorial normalization.
- **step-projective-log-pullback** [claim](#stmt-p1logcbfalt-log-pullback) / [proof](#proof-stmt-p1logcbfalt-log-pullback): Pull back logarithmic differentials and take exterior and tensor powers. -> Injective pullback of divisible logarithmic pluricanonical sections.
- **step-projective-relative-iitaka** [claim](#stmt-p1logcbfalt-iitaka) / [proof](#proof-stmt-p1logcbfalt-iitaka): Apply the Iitaka theorem over the base function field, spread out, resolve and compare full logarithmic section systems. -> A relative Iitaka fibration with geometrically connected generic fiber and Kodaira-zero Iitaka fibers.
- **step-easy-upper-addition** [claim](#stmt-p1logcbfalt-easy-addition) / [proof](#proof-stmt-p1logcbfalt-easy-addition): Bound the joint image of the base map and each complete divisor system. -> The elementary upper addition bound and nonvanishing after general-fiber restriction.

          "anchor": "stmt-rankone-comparison",
          "exact_quote": "Let $g:(X,\\Delta)\\to W$ be a fibration with connected fibers from a smooth compact Kähler manifold to a smooth compact manifold in class $\\mathcal C$. After the modifications of [setup:log-modification](#setup:log-modification), [setup:flattening](#setup:flattening), fix a smooth compact reference pair $(X_0,\\Delta_0)$ and a modification $$\\pi:X\\longrightarrow X_0,\\qquad\n \\Delta=\\pi_*^{-1}\\Delta_0+\\operatorname{Exc}(\\pi)_{\\mathrm{red}},$$ such that every prime divisor mapped by $g$ into codimension at least two is $\\pi$-exceptional. Then there are a rational line bundle $M$ and a rational divisor $T$ on $W$ with the following properties.*\n\n1.  *A positive integral multiple of $M$ is the parabolic highest Hodge line of a complex direct summand of a pure, real-polarizable variation with an integral lattice on a dense open subset of $W$. If every prime over a prime $D$ of $W$ has $\\Delta$-coefficient one, then $T_D=1$.*\n\n\n3.  *In sufficiently divisible degrees $q$, multiplication by the relative generating forms gives linear isomorphisms $$\\label{rankone:sections}\n     H^0\\bigl(W,q(K_W+T+M)\\bigr)\n     \\simeq H^0\\bigl(X,q(K_X+\\Delta)\\bigr)\n     \\simeq H^0\\bigl(X_0,q(K_{X_0}+\\Delta_0)\\bigr).$$ In a common divisible grading they preserve products and ratios of sections, and hence the dimensions of the images of their linear systems.*\n\n\n4.  *The same comparison holds relatively. If $h:W\\to Y$ and $f_0:X_0\\to Y$ are holomorphic maps with $f_0\\pi=hg$, then $$\\label{rankone:relative-sections}\n     h_*\\mathcal O_W\\bigl(q(K_W+T+M)\\bigr)\n     \\simeq (f_0)_*\\mathcal O_{X_0}\\bigl(q(K_{X_0}+\\Delta_0)\\bigr)$$ for sufficiently divisible $q$. <a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **inv-orbifold-subadditivity**: The total log canonical Kodaira dimension is bounded below by the sum of the very general fiber contribution and the invariant orbifold base contribution.
- **inv-logarithmic-model-invariance**: Adding the reduced exceptional divisor to the strict transform of the boundary preserves divisible section rings, their multiplication, and their Iitaka dimensions.
- **inv-character-line-scope**: The tautological form spans the root-character part of the highest Hodge space; this assertion concerns the character part rather than the entire highest space.
- **inv-projective-adjoint-descent**: After modification, the parabolic highest Hodge line descends from a nef rational line on a smooth projective quotient, where adding a positive rational multiple of that line to the canonical divisor gives a big divisor.
- **inv-marked-boundary-rankloss**: Restriction of the descended nef line to each marked boundary component strictly decreases its numerical dimension.
- **inv-reduced-minimal-root-cover**: For a reduced logarithmic Kodaira-zero projective pair, the minimal root cover is geometrically integral and every positive-degree logarithmic pluricanonical space is generated by the corresponding power of its root form.
- **inv-normalized-model-sections**: Prescribed smooth modifications of the normalized base preserve sufficiently divisible adjoint section spaces when the boundary is the strict transform plus the reduced exceptional divisor and the moduli divisor is pulled back.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **anti-finite-degree-certificate**: Treat the subadditivity theorem as an effective procedure that computes complete pluricanonical systems in a prescribed degree. -> The comparison is asserted in sufficiently divisible degrees, without a finite degree bound or an executable representation of the compact geometric input.
- **anti-unresolved-prime-tests**: Compute the parabolic order using only the original central prime and omit exceptional primes created during resolution. -> A prime introduced by resolving a tangency can determine the parabolic order and is missed by the original central prime.
- **anti-entire-top-space-for-rational-boundary**: Apply the entire highest-space conclusion of the reduced-boundary root-cover theorem to a general rational boundary. -> The entire top-form space can have larger dimension even though the distinguished character line remains available.
- **anti-identify-line-and-full-period-ranks**: Replace the retained full period map by the map remembering only the chosen highest line when constructing the quotient. -> The chosen-line map can have smaller rank, so its rank does not establish generic immersivity of the full retained periods.
- **anti-discard-finite-monodromy-boundary**: Mark only boundary components with infinite local monodromy when removing the period boundary. -> Finite nonidentity monodromy components also belong to the marked divisor and require the rank-loss argument.
- **anti-logarithmic-base-with-missing-pullback**: Use the logarithmic Kodaira dimension of a prescribed base boundary when its inverse support is omitted from the source boundary. -> The hypothesis giving the comparison with the prescribed logarithmic base is missing.
- **anti-extrapolate-field-characteristic**: Apply the projective ordinary or logarithmic specialization over a field of positive characteristic. -> The stated field-extension conclusion has no such characteristic regime.
- **anti-conflate-normalized-model-rules**: Identify a recomputed minimum-rule discriminant on a higher base with the prescribed strict boundary plus reduced exceptional divisor. -> Recomputation introduces a base twist containing the canonical discrepancy; it is a different operation from prescribing the exceptional boundary.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `not_applicable`. The principal results concern compact complex fibrations, very general fibers, existential modifications and asymptotic section systems, rather than a finite constructive input-output problem. The rank-one comparison states: "In a common divisible grading they preserve products and ratios of sections, and hence the dimensions of the images of their linear systems." The relative Iitaka proof explicitly states: "No finite generation is required." The finite divisorial arithmetic in the proofs does not decide the geometric hypotheses or certify the global subadditivity theorem.

<a id="pm-s5"></a>
## 5. Domain engineering contracts

| domain | applies | strength | maturity | basis | why |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| software | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The subadditivity theorem concerns holomorphic fibrations of compact complex manifolds. It supplies no construction assigning its geometric objects or Kodaira dimensions to software components. |
| games_simulation | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The relative Iitaka construction produces a geometric factorization characterized on very general fibers. It supplies neither a simulation state nor an evolution rule for a game or simulated system. |
| tools | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The divisorial section test characterizes membership through inequalities at every prime divisor. It does not supply a finite representation or an executable procedure for obtaining all the required valuations. |
| algorithms | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The boundary-removal lemma proves bigness for sufficiently large rational coefficients through asymptotic section counts. It gives no explicit threshold or termination bound for finding such a coefficient. |
| data_structures | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The finite invariant-algebra construction preserves geometric image dimensions and pole bounds. The argument does not specify an encoding, supported operations, or storage guarantees for a data structure. |
| hardware_accelerators | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Curvature and numerical dimension are related through Hodge metrics and intersection theory. These objects supply no hardware layout, arithmetic kernel specification, or accelerator execution model. |
| crypto_security | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The cyclic root cover is constructed to realize logarithmic top forms of a geometric pair. Its deck action and root extraction carry no stated secrecy, hardness, or adversarial security contract. |
| machine_learning | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The period quotient descends a Hodge variation and its highest-weight line to a projective variety. It supplies no learning objective, training procedure, or predictive guarantee. |
| optimisation_operations_research | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The adjoint-addition argument combines section systems and cancels an ample twist while preserving their ratios. This establishes a geometric dimension bound rather than an optimisation problem with a feasible set and an algorithm. |
| distributed_systems | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Descent of local Hodge lines uses tensor invariants to eliminate deck-transformation ambiguity in geometric overlap data. It does not define a distributed protocol or address communication failures. |
| other (Algebraic and complex geometry) | True | strong | adaptable | inferred | The paper constructs relative Iitaka factorizations and identifies complete divisible adjoint section systems through a Hodge line and a boundary on the intermediate base. These constructions support mathematical comparisons of fibrations, with no effective computational procedure asserted. |

The algebra is integral and finite over $\\mathcal B^G$. Indeed each homogeneous generator satisfies its orbit polynomial with invariant coefficients, and invariants of a finite group acting on a finitely generated algebra are finitely generated. The corresponding map $\\operatorname{Proj}\\mathcal B\\to\\operatorname{Proj}\\mathcal B^G$ is finite. Before taking invariants, the system contains the sections pulled back from $\\mathcal V$, whose restriction to a general source fiber has image dimension $\\dim F$. A finite map preserves the dimension of the image of that fiber, even though $G$ may permute the fibers above one point of $Y$. Choose a Veronese degree for $\\mathcal B^G$ generated by finitely many elements of that single degree. Their ratios therefore still have rank $\\dim F$ on general fibers. Denote their pluricanonical degree by $b_0$. The same finite quotient also preserves the total image dimension, which is at least $\\dim F+\\dim R$.\n\nUse the natural identification of relative canonical bundles on the common smooth locus for every base change or modification. On the dense étale locus, invariant relative tensors therefore descend to $V$. They extend meromorphically across the remaining locus by finite analytic descent. The horizontal bounds descend because the comparison cover is generically étale along each horizontal divisor.\n\nFor completeness, the vertical order calculation is exact. Let $E'$ lie over $E$, with ramification index $e$. For a degree-$b_0$ top pluriform $\\alpha$, $$\\label{generaltype:ramified-order}\n  \\mathop{\\mathrm{ord}}_{E'}(\\alpha_{\\mathrm{pullback}})+b_0\n       =e\\bigl(\\mathop{\\mathrm{ord}}_E(\\alpha)+b_0\\bigr).$$ A logarithmic top form on the base pulls back to a nonvanishing logarithmic top form at a prime dominating its divisor. Apply [generaltype:ramified-order](#generaltype:ramified-order) to the relative form wedged with this base logarithmic form. The bound upstairs is therefore equivalent to [generaltype:relative-log-bound](#generaltype:relative-log-bound) downstairs. For (iii), pull the chosen Galois comparison cover and its original sections to a common refinement over the modified base. Lemma <a href=\"#generaltype:stable-orders\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"generaltype:stable-orders\">27</a> applies to these same pullbacks at primes above the newly exposed base divisor; its bounds pass to their conjugates, products, and invariant combinations. The ramification calculation therefore gives the bound for the pullback of each fixed descended member of $\\mathcal S$. Products add the order inequalities, and taking a full product system is a Veronese operation on its image, so products preserve the relative rank as well.",
          "source_object": "proof-stmt-generaltype-relative-system"
        }
      ],

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **limit-compact-class-c-geometric-scope** (hypothesis): The main inequality requires the stated smooth compact connected class-C source, rational SNC boundary, and surjective holomorphic map with connected fibers onto a normal compact irreducible base. Blocked use: ['Applying the main theorem to arbitrary compact complex spaces without establishing its source and base hypotheses.', 'Interpreting a software dependency map as a qualifying geometric fibration.']
- **limit-boundary-coefficients-and-support** (hypothesis): The original orbifold boundary must have rational coefficients in the prescribed interval and simple normal crossing support. The later auxiliary subpairs permitting negative coefficients do not enlarge this original hypothesis. Blocked use: ['Applying the main theorem directly to an arbitrary negative subboundary.', 'Ignoring boundary singularities on the supplied source model.']
- **limit-very-general-fiber** (formal_scope): Fiber conclusions concern very general smooth fibers, rather than every special or singular fiber. Blocked use: ['Assigning the asserted fiber Kodaira dimension to a prescribed singular fiber.', 'Dropping the exclusions needed for generic base change and boundary-stratum control.']
- **limit-invariant-base-versus-chosen-model** (formal_scope): The main base contribution is the invariant over allowed equivalent models. A chosen smooth model supplies a comparison in the stated direction, rather than an automatic identification with that invariant. Blocked use: ['Replacing the invariant term by a larger chosen-model base Kodaira dimension without a separate justification.']
- **limit-logarithmic-modification-rule** (hypothesis): Section-ring invariance uses the strict transform of the boundary plus the reduced exceptional divisor, with the resulting boundary SNC and degrees clearing the rational coefficients. Blocked use: ['Claiming invariance after assigning arbitrary exceptional coefficients.', 'Applying the sheaf identity in a degree where the required divisor multiples are not integral.']
- **limit-flattening-does-not-survive-resolution-as-flatness** (formal_scope): Flattening supplies an equidimensional intermediate family. The resolved source retains exceptionality over the reference source; its flatness is not asserted. Blocked use: ['Using flatness of the final resolved source without an additional proof.']
- **limit-relative-iitaka-nonnegative-fiber** (hypothesis): The relative Iitaka reduction assumes nonnegative logarithmic Kodaira dimension on very general smooth fibers. Blocked use: ['Invoking the stated factorization when the fiber has no nonzero log pluricanonical systems.']
- **limit-no-effective-stabilized-degree** (nonconstructive): The relative Iitaka argument chooses a sufficiently divisible stabilized degree. It supplies no explicit degree threshold or finite stopping criterion for finding that degree. Blocked use: ['Presenting the reduction as a degree-bounded algorithm.', 'Certifying the asymptotic Kodaira dimension from an arbitrary finite sample of section spaces.']
- **limit-fixed-reference-exceptional-descent** (hypothesis): The forward rank-one section comparison needs every source prime contracted into higher base codimension to be exceptional over the fixed smooth reference pair. Blocked use: ['Extending a base section to the original source after testing only primes dominating base divisors, without controlling the remaining primes.']
- **limit-character-line-not-entire-top-space** (counterexample_scope): For general rational boundaries, the root-character part is a line. The entire top-form space need not be a line; the stronger refinement requires a reduced boundary. Blocked use: ['Replacing a character eigenspace by the entire highest Hodge space for an arbitrary rational boundary.']
- **limit-prepared-source-prime-coverage** (hypothesis): The parabolic order calculation includes exceptional primes introduced when preparing the resolved source. The original central prime alone can miss the controlling order. Blocked use: ['Computing the parabolic order solely from the unresolved central fiber.']
- **limit-hodge-positivity-ambient-lattice** (hypothesis): Adjoint positivity requires a pure real-polarizable variation with an integral ambient lattice and a complex direct summand whose highest nonzero Hodge step is a line. Blocked use: ['Applying adjoint positivity to an arbitrary nef line without its Hodge realization.', 'Requiring the chosen complex summand itself to carry the rational structure that the theorem places on the ambient variation.']
- **limit-line-map-and-full-period-map** (formal_scope): The chosen-line map and the full period map can have different ranks. The quotient construction uses retained full periods; numerical dimension of the chosen line measures the line map. Blocked use: ['Inferring generic immersivity of the full period map from an unjustified identification of these ranks.']
- **limit-marked-boundary-includes-finite-monodromy** (hypothesis): The marked period boundary includes components with finite nonidentity local monodromy. Boundary removal requires strict numerical rank loss on every marked component. Blocked use: ['Removing only the infinite-monodromy boundary while silently discarding finite nonidentity components.']
- **limit-boundary-subtraction-asymptotic-regime** (nonconstructive): Boundary subtraction assumes bigness before removal and strict numerical dimension loss on each boundary component. Its conclusion concerns sufficiently large rational nef coefficients, without an explicit uniform threshold. Blocked use: ['Dropping the strict restriction-rank hypothesis.', 'Claiming bigness for an arbitrary prescribed small coefficient.']
- **limit-stable-comparison-klt-horizontal-boundary** (hypothesis): The stable-family comparison uses an effective horizontal rational SNC boundary with coefficients strictly below one and a big adjoint on positive-dimensional very general fibers. Blocked use: ["Applying this comparison directly to a coefficient-one horizontal boundary without the proof's preliminary reduction.", 'Applying stable-family positivity to an arbitrary family lacking the stated stable and klt conditions.']
- **limit-valuation-bounds-on-higher-base-models** (formal_scope): The relative forms must retain logarithmic bounds at divisors exposed after modification of the base. Testing only the original base divisors does not discharge this requirement. Blocked use: ['Leaving poles unchecked at source divisors whose original images have higher codimension.']
- **limit-generaltype-addition-boundary-conditions** (hypothesis): General-type addition requires a big fiber log canonical divisor, domination of the prescribed base boundary by the inf-multiplicity boundary, and coefficient one at source divisors contracted into higher base codimension. Blocked use: ['Adding the fiber dimension when the fiber adjoint is merely nonnegative.', 'Omitting the contracted-divisor boundary condition from the application.']
- **limit-weak-effectivity-is-existential** (nonconstructive): Weak effectivity assumes a fixed nonzero divisible fiber system and a big rational base twist. It asserts a nonzero section in some positive degree, without computing that degree or supplying generation of a complete system. Blocked use: ['Replacing the big twist by an arbitrary nef twist.', 'Reading nonvanishing as global generation or an effective section-construction algorithm.']
- **limit-adjoint-addition-needs-both-fibrations** (hypothesis): Adjoint addition requires the nef moduli term to descend from a projective base with the stated big canonical twist, together with fiberwise bigness for the original intermediate fibration. Blocked use: ['Applying the addition principle to an arbitrary nef moduli term without the projective descent data.', 'Assuming a map between the original base and the period base.']
- **limit-logarithmic-pullback-support** (hypothesis): The prescribed logarithmic base term requires every prime component of its pullback to have source boundary coefficient one. Blocked use: ['Using the prescribed logarithmic base contribution when its inverse boundary support is omitted from the source.']
- **limit-characteristic-zero-specialization** (hypothesis): The field-extension specialization assumes an algebraically closed field of characteristic zero and uses the geometric generic fiber. Blocked use: ['Applying the characteristic-zero corollary over a positive-characteristic field.', 'Substituting an arbitrary special fiber for the geometric generic fiber.']
- **limit-core-stable-representatives** (formal_scope): Core and tower fiber properties use compatible strictly neat and high representatives. The section comparisons do not assert preservation of every stable-fiber property under arbitrary model changes. Blocked use: ['Transferring stable orbifold fiber properties through an arbitrary birational diagram using only section-ring invariance.']
- **limit-specialness-converse-not-proved** (formal_scope): The core corollary implies specialness from logarithmic Kodaira dimension zero. It does not establish the converse. Blocked use: ['Concluding logarithmic Kodaira dimension zero solely from specialness.']
- **limit-reduced-minimal-root-cover** (hypothesis): The entire logarithmic pluriform-space conclusion uses a reduced SNC boundary, geometric logarithmic Kodaira dimension zero, and the least positive root index. Blocked use: ['Replacing the minimal root cover by an arbitrary higher-degree cover while retaining the geometric integrality conclusion.', 'Extending the entire-space refinement to a nonreduced rational boundary.']
- **limit-moduli-identification-not-semiampleness** (formal_scope): Identification of the Hodge line with the moduli divisor permits negative coefficients in the auxiliary generic subpair. It does not invoke the separate semiampleness theorem requiring generic effectiveness. Blocked use: ['Inferring semiampleness solely from the moduli identification when generic effectiveness has not been established.']
- **limit-distinct-higher-model-boundary-rules** (formal_scope): Prescribing strict boundary plus reduced exceptional boundary is distinct from recomputing the minimum-rule discriminant on a higher base. The latter comparison includes the canonical discrepancy of the base. Blocked use: ['Identifying recomputed discriminant coefficients with the prescribed exceptional boundary without checking the model twist.']
- **limit-divisorial-test-not-finite-enumeration** (formal_scope): The section-membership characterization requires normality and inequalities at every prime divisor. It supplies no general finite enumeration procedure for arbitrary geometric inputs. Blocked use: ['Certifying a section from an incomplete selection of valuations.', 'Treating the characterization as an executable decision procedure without an independently justified finite reduction.']

Suppose that $\\Delta$ is an SNC boundary with rational coefficients in $[0,1]$ and that $\\kappa(G,K_G+\\Delta_G)=0$ on a very general smooth fiber $G$. After the modifications of [setup:log-modification](#setup:log-modification), [setup:flattening](#setup:flattening), fix a smooth compact reference pair $(X_0,\\Delta_0)$ and a modification $$\\pi:X\\longrightarrow X_0,\\qquad\n \\Delta=\\pi_*^{-1}\\Delta_0+\\operatorname{Exc}(\\pi)_{\\mathrm{red}},$$ such that every prime divisor mapped by $g$ into codimension at least two is $\\pi$-exceptional. Then there are a rational line bundle $M$ and a rational divisor $T$ on $W$ with the following properties.*\n\n1.  *A positive integral multiple of $M$ is the parabolic highest Hodge line of a complex direct summand of a pure, real-polarizable variation with an integral lattice on a dense open subset of $W$. Its parabolic extension is the line to which the adjoint-positivity theorem, [period:adjoint](#period:adjoint), will apply.*\n\n\n2.  *The divisor $T$ is supported on an SNC divisor and satisfies $$\\label{rankone:boundary}\n     B(g,\\Delta)\\leq T\\leq 1.$$ In particular, $T$ is effective. If every prime over a prime $D$ of $W$ has $\\Delta$-coefficient one, then $T_D=1$.*\n\n\n3.  *In sufficiently divisible degrees $q$, multiplication by the relative generating forms gives linear isomorphisms $$\\label{rankone:sections}\n     H^0\\bigl(W,q(K_W+T+M)\\bigr)\n     \\simeq H^0\\bigl(X,q(K_X+\\Delta)\\bigr)\n     \\simeq H^0\\bigl(X_0,q(K_{X_0}+\\Delta_0)\\bigr).$$ In a common divisible grading they preserve products and ratios of sections, and hence the dimensions of the images of their linear systems.*\n\n\n4.  *The same comparison holds relatively. If $h:W\\to Y$ and $f_0:X_0\\to Y$ are holomorphic maps with $f_0\\pi=hg$, then $$\\label{rankone:relative-sections}\n     h_*\\mathcal O_W\\bigl(q(K_W+T+M)\\bigr)\n     \\simeq (f_0)_*\\mathcal O_{X_0}\\bigl(q(K_{X_0}+\\Delta_0)\\bigr)$$ for sufficiently divisible $q$. The comparison of ratios also applies to systems over $Y$ and to their restrictions wherever generic base change holds. Choose the least positive integer $p$ for which $H^0(J,p(K_J+D))\\ne0$, a nonzero section $\\omega$ in that space, and put $$E=\\operatorname{div}(\\omega)+pD,\n \\qquad \\Delta=D-\\frac1pE.$$ Write $\\omega=b\\eta^{\\otimes p}$ in a rational canonical frame, and let $\\tau:N\\to J$ be the normalization in $K(J)[t]/(t^p-b)$. Let $\\rho:\\widetilde N\\to N$ be a log resolution, and set $$P=\\operatorname{Supp}(\\Delta^{=1}),\\qquad\n D_{\\widetilde N}=\\bigl((\\tau\\circ\\rho)^{-1}P\\bigr)_{\\mathrm{red}}.$$ Then $N$ is geometrically integral, the rational top form $\\Omega=t\\tau^*\\eta$ extends logarithmically to $(\\widetilde N,D_{\\widetilde N})$, and $$H^0\\bigl(\\widetilde N,m(K_{\\widetilde N}+D_{\\widetilde N})\\bigr)\n       =K\\,\\Omega^m\\qquad(m\\ge1).$$",

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


We prove Campana's orbifold Iitaka subadditivity conjecture for rational simple normal crossing boundaries on compact manifolds in Fujiki class $`\mathcal C`$, including coefficient one. Ordinary and logarithmic subadditivity follow.

<a id="intro:section"></a>
### Introduction

The Kodaira dimension measures the growth of pluricanonical forms. For a surjective morphism $f:X\to Y$ of smooth projective complex varieties with connected fibers, Iitaka’s subadditivity problem asks whether $$\kappa(X)\geq\kappa(F)+\kappa(Y),$$ where $F$ is a general fiber. The logarithmic version permits poles along reduced boundary divisors and therefore applies to open varieties. We prove a common generalization in which rational boundary coefficients and multiple fibers both contribute to the inequality.

Iitaka’s divisor-dimension and logarithmic theories provide the language for this question (Iitaka 1971, 1977b) <a id="pm-cite-1"></a>[[IitakaOriginal](#ref-IitakaOriginal), [IitakaLog](#ref-IitakaLog)]; an explicit logarithmic subadditivity conjecture appears in (Iitaka 1977a, sec. 3) <a id="pm-cite-2"></a>[[IitakaClassification](#ref-IitakaClassification)]. The lower bound requires global pluricanonical forms that retain the growth of both the fibers and the base. Direct-image positivity established central cases: Kawamata treated curve bases and fibers admitting good minimal models, Viehweg developed weak positivity and addition over a base of general type, and Kollár proved addition for general-type fibers (Kawamata 1982, 1985; Viehweg 1983; Kollár 1987) <a id="pm-cite-3"></a>[[KawamataCurves](#ref-KawamataCurves), [KawamataKodaira](#ref-KawamataKodaira), [ViehwegAdditivity](#ref-ViehwegAdditivity), [Kollar87](#ref-Kollar87)]. Weak positivity controls symmetric powers after small positive ample twists; a general-type base provides enough canonical positivity to absorb them.

Other advances exploit dimension or the geometry of the base: Birkar treated total dimension at most six, Cao–Păun and Hacon–Popa–Schnell treated abelian or maximal-Albanese-dimension bases, and Cao treated projective klt pairs over surfaces (Birkar 2009; Cao and Păun 2017; Hacon, Popa, and Schnell 2018; Cao 2018) <a id="pm-cite-4"></a>[[BirkarIitakaSix](#ref-BirkarIitakaSix), [CaoPaunAbelian](#ref-CaoPaunAbelian), [HaconPopaSchnellAbelian](#ref-HaconPopaSchnellAbelian), [CaoSurfaces](#ref-CaoSurfaces)]. Thus the established cases draw positivity from different parts of the fibration, rather than imposing one uniform condition on it.

For logarithmic pairs, Kovács–Patakfalvi proved the projective log-general-type-fiber case (Kovács and Patakfalvi 2017, Theorem 9.6) <a id="pm-cite-5"></a>[[KovacsPatakfalvi](#ref-KovacsPatakfalvi)], while Hashizume proved reduced-SNC subadditivity for fibers with abundant log canonical divisor (Hashizume 2020, Theorem 1.2) <a id="pm-cite-6"></a>[[Hashizume](#ref-Hashizume)]. Maehara had established logarithmic addition over a base of log general type; Fujino gives a proof through twisted weak positivity (Fujino 2017, Theorem 1.9) <a id="pm-cite-7"></a>[[FujinoWeakPositivity](#ref-FujinoWeakPositivity)]. Campana proved addition over an orbifold base of general type, and Wang proved klt Kähler subadditivity over complex tori, including an inf-multiplicity refinement (Campana 2011, Theorem 6.3) <a id="pm-cite-8"></a>[[CampanaOrbifolds](#ref-CampanaOrbifolds)] <a id="pm-cite-9"></a>[[WangKahlerIitaka](#ref-WangKahlerIitaka)] (Wang 2021, Main Theorem A and Theorem E). The present argument uses general-type addition only after a relative Iitaka reduction; the original fiber need not be of general type or admit a good minimal model.

Earlier preprints also state general ordinary inequalities. Tsuji states ordinary subadditivity following a direct-image generation theorem (Tsuji 2010, Theorems 1.9 and 1.13) <a id="pm-cite-10"></a>[[TsujiDirectImages](#ref-TsujiDirectImages)]; his Remark 1.14 distinguishes an unproved variation refinement. Maehara states an ordinary determinant inequality involving variation (Maehara 2010, sec. 7, Theorem 8) <a id="pm-cite-11"></a>[[MaeharaIitaka](#ref-MaeharaIitaka)]. This later preprint claim is distinct from his logarithmic general-type-base theorem above. Neither preprint statement is a proof input here.

<a id="pm-h-3"></a>
#### The orbifold base and the main theorem

Multiple fibers carry information that the ordinary base omits. Campana’s orbifold base records it through inf multiplicities, and his birational invariant gives the corresponding form of Iitaka’s conjecture (Campana 2004, 2011) <a id="pm-cite-12"></a>[[Campana2004](#ref-Campana2004), [CampanaOrbifolds](#ref-CampanaOrbifolds)]. We specify that invariant before stating the theorem.

<span id="setup:orbifold-base" label="setup:orbifold-base"></span> A fibration is a proper surjective holomorphic map with connected fibers. A rational SNC boundary on a smooth manifold is a finite sum $\Delta=\sum_i\delta_i\Delta_i$, with $\delta_i\in\mathbf Q\cap[0,1]$ and simple normal crossing support. Write $\Delta_E$ for the coefficient of a source prime $E$. Set $$m_\Delta(E)=\frac1{1-\Delta_E},
 \qquad \frac1{0}=\infty,$$ where $\Delta_E=0$ off the boundary. If the base of $f:(X,\Delta)\to Y$ is smooth, define <a id="setup:inf-boundary"></a>$$\label{setup:inf-boundary}
\begin{gathered}
 m(f,\Delta;D)=\min_{E\mapsto D}
     \bigl\{\operatorname{ord}_E(f^*D)m_\Delta(E)\bigr\},
 \qquad
 B(f,\Delta)=\sum_D\left(1-\frac1{m(f,\Delta;D)}\right)D.
\end{gathered}$$ Here $D$ ranges over base prime divisors, $E$ over source primes dominating $D$, and $1/\infty=0$. The minimum is nonempty and only finitely many coefficients of $B(f,\Delta)$ are nonzero. There is no divisibility condition on these multiplicities.

<a id="setup:orbifold-morphism"></a>
For smooth sources and bases, an elementary change of model is a commutative diagram of fibrations $$\begin{tikzcd}
 (X',\Delta') \arrow[r,"u"] \arrow[d,"f'"'] &
 (X,\Delta) \arrow[d,"f"]\\
 Y' \arrow[r,"v"'] & Y
\end{tikzcd}$$ where $u,v$ are proper bimeromorphic holomorphic maps, $u_*\Delta'=\Delta$, and both boundaries are rational SNC boundaries in $[0,1]$. We require, for every prime $D$ on $X$ and prime $E$ on $X'$ with $t=\operatorname{ord}_E(u^*D)>0$, $$\label{setup:orbifold-morphism}
                     t\,m_{\Delta'}(E)\geq m_\Delta(D).$$ We permit $\infty\geq\infty$ and impose no condition for $t=0$. Write $f'\sim f$ for the equivalence relation generated by these diagrams, allowing arrows in either direction. Define <a id="setup:invariant-base"></a>$$\label{setup:invariant-base}
 \kappa(f\mid\Delta)=
 \inf_{f':(X',\Delta')\to Y'\sim f}
       \kappa\bigl(Y',K_{Y'}+B(f',\Delta')\bigr).$$ For a normal singular base $Y$, resolve $Y$, resolve the main component of the fiber product with $X$, and give the resulting smooth source the strict transform of $\Delta$ plus the reduced exceptional divisor over $X$. After resolving this boundary, apply the preceding definition. It is independent of the chosen resolution (Campana 2011, Definitions 2.1, 2.3, 3.2, 4.7, and 4.10; Remark 4.13 and Corollary 4.14) <a id="pm-cite-13"></a>[[CampanaOrbifolds](#ref-CampanaOrbifolds)].

For a rational line bundle $L$, $\kappa(Z,L)$ is the maximal image dimension of its complete linear systems in sufficiently divisible positive degrees, and is $-\infty$ if all these systems are empty. A point has Kodaira dimension zero, and $(-\infty)+a=-\infty$, including $a=-\infty$. Fujiki class $\mathcal C$ consists of compact complex spaces bimeromorphic to compact Kähler spaces.

<a id="stmt-main-orbifold"></a>
<div id="main:orbifold" class="theorem">

**Theorem 1** (Orbifold subadditivity). *Let $X$ be a smooth compact connected complex manifold in Fujiki class $\mathcal C$, let $\Delta$ be a rational SNC boundary, and let $f:X\to Y$ be a surjective holomorphic map with connected fibers onto a normal compact irreducible complex space. For a very general smooth fiber $F$, put $\Delta_F=\Delta|_F$. Then <a id="main:inequality"></a>$$\label{main:inequality}
 \kappa(X,K_X+\Delta)
 \geq\kappa(F,K_F+\Delta_F)+\kappa(f\mid\Delta).$$*

</div>

This is a positive resolution of Campana’s orbifold Iitaka conjecture in its rational SNC, Fujiki-class-$\mathcal C$ formulation, including coefficient one (Campana 2011, Conjecture 6.1 and Remark 6.2) <a id="pm-cite-14"></a>[[CampanaOrbifolds](#ref-CampanaOrbifolds)]. Here a very general fiber is chosen outside a countable union of proper closed analytic subsets, including the critical values and the exceptional images of boundary strata.

For reduced SNC divisors $D_X,D_Y$ satisfying $\operatorname{Supp}(f^*D_Y)\subseteq\operatorname{Supp}(D_X)$, the invariant base term is at least $\kappa(Y,K_Y+D_Y)$. Theorem <a href="#main:orbifold" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="main:orbifold">1</a> therefore gives $$\kappa(X,K_X+D_X)
 \geq\kappa(F,K_F+D_X|_F)+\kappa(Y,K_Y+D_Y)$$ for fibrations between smooth compact class-$\mathcal C$ manifolds. For a smooth quasi-projective variety $U$, its logarithmic Kodaira dimension is $\bar\kappa(U)=\kappa(X,K_X+D_X)$ on a smooth projective compactification with reduced SNC boundary $D_X=X\setminus U$. Compatible compactifications give logarithmic subadditivity for dominant morphisms of smooth quasi-projective complex varieties with geometrically connected general fiber. Empty boundaries give ordinary subadditivity. The projective ordinary and logarithmic statements also hold over algebraically closed fields of characteristic zero, using geometric generic fibers.

<a id="pm-h-4"></a>
#### The reduction and the two positivity inputs

The proof first replaces the original fiber by a general-type object without losing its complete pluricanonical systems. Assume the two terms on the right of Theorem <a href="#main:orbifold" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="main:orbifold">1</a> are nonnegative, and put $k=\kappa(F,K_F+\Delta_F)$. A relative Iitaka map gives, after the model changes of Section <a href="#setup:section" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="setup:section">2</a>, $$X\xrightarrow{g}W\xrightarrow{h}Y,
 \qquad \dim W_y=k,
 \qquad\kappa(G,K_G+\Delta_G)=0$$ for a very general $g$-fiber $G$.

The canonical-bundle-formula strategy separates the contribution of singular fibers from a moduli contribution on $W$. Fujino–Mori developed a higher-dimensional logarithmic formula, and Ambro organized its discriminant and moduli parts birationally (Fujino and Mori 2000; Ambro 2004, 2005) <a id="pm-cite-15"></a>[[FujinoMori](#ref-FujinoMori), [AmbroBoundary](#ref-AmbroBoundary), [AmbroModuli](#ref-AmbroModuli)]. Our use of this strategy requires an exact comparison on the fixed original source. Section <a href="#setup:section" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="setup:section">2</a> obtains it from the orders of relative pluriforms. A sufficiently divisible pluricanonical system on $G$ is one-dimensional. Taking a root of its generator on a cyclic cover gives a differential form, and the line it spans belongs to the cohomology of that cover. Tensor descent and extension across the boundary produce a rational line bundle $M$ on $W$. The orders of the same form determine a boundary $T$ such that $$B(g,\Delta)\leq T\leq1,
 \qquad
 H^0\bigl(X,m(K_X+\Delta)\bigr)
 \simeq H^0\bigl(W,m(K_W+T+M)\bigr)$$ in sufficiently divisible degrees. The comparison also holds relatively over $Y$. Thus $K_W+T+M$ is big on the $k$-dimensional fibers of $h$.

Two inputs turn this fiberwise bigness into the required global lower bound. The first concerns the line $M$: the period map, which records how the Hodge structures of the covers vary, supplies a second fibration $p:W\to S$, with $S$ smooth projective, and a nef rational line bundle $P$ such that $$M=p^*P,\qquad K_S+aP\ \text{is big}$$ for some positive rational $a$. The maps $h$ and $p$ have different jobs, as Figure <a href="#fig:iitaka-period" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="fig:iitaka-period">1</a> shows. The second input is log-general-type addition: a log canonical divisor that is big on the fibers of $h$ has Kodaira dimension at least their dimension plus the orbifold contribution of $Y$.

<figure id="fig:iitaka-period">

<figcaption>The relative Iitaka reduction factors the original fibration through $W$. The map $h$ retains the original base and has $k$-dimensional general fibers. The independent map $p$ expresses $M$ as the pullback of a nef line $P$ on a projective base where $K_{S} + aP$ is big. No map between $Y$ and $S$ is asserted.</figcaption>
</figure>

<a id="pm-figsrc-fig-iitaka-period"></a>
**Figure source and visual witness** (`fig:iitaka-period`)

Visual witness description of PDF page 4 (generated from the PDF page, not source text):

```text
A commutative diagram with four objects: $X$, $W$, $Y$, and $S$. A horizontal rightward arrow labeled $g$ goes from $X$ to $W$. A horizontal rightward arrow labeled $h$ goes from $W$ to $Y$. A vertical downward arrow labeled $p$ goes from $W$ to $S$.
```


Section <a href="#addition:section" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="addition:section">3</a> states both inputs precisely and proves the intervening addition principle. Write $D_W=K_W+T$. For a fixed rational $c<1$ close to one, general-type addition gives sufficiently many ratios of sections of $D_W+cM+\eta p^*A$, where $A$ is ample on $S$ and $\eta>0$ is rational. Weak positivity then supplies one fixed section of $D_W+aM-\lambda p^*A$ for suitable $a>1$ and $\lambda>0$. A positive rational combination cancels the ample terms and gives coefficient one on $M$. Multiplication by powers of that fixed section preserves the ratios of the first system. This is an exact section argument; it requires neither abundance of $P$ nor a limiting assertion for Kodaira dimensions.

The full proofs of the inputs follow this calculation. Section <a href="#period:section" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="period:section">4</a> proves adjoint positivity of the Hodge line. It combines the curvature and boundary theory of Griffiths, Schmid, and Cattani–Kaplan–Schmid with a projective period quotient (Griffiths 1970; Schmid 1973; Cattani, Kaplan, and Schmid 1986) <a id="pm-cite-16"></a>[[GriffithsPeriodsIII](#ref-GriffithsPeriodsIII), [Schmid](#ref-Schmid), [CKS](#ref-CKS)]. The construction retains suitable rational adjoint factors of the ambient integral variation, associated with its monodromy, from which a positive power of the chosen line can be recovered. Their full periods give the quotient, using Villadsen’s class-$\mathcal C$ compactification theorem and the period-quotient approach of Bakker–Brunebarbe–Tsimerman and Sommese; curvature and a volume argument establish its projectivity (Bakker, Brunebarbe, and Tsimerman 2023; Sommese 1978; Villadsen 2024) <a id="pm-cite-17"></a>[[BakkerBrunebarbeTsimerman](#ref-BakkerBrunebarbeTsimerman), [SommesePeriodMapping](#ref-SommesePeriodMapping), [VilladsenHodgeKahler](#ref-VilladsenHodgeKahler)].

On this quotient $S$, the retained full period map is generically immersive, but the map remembering only the chosen line may have smaller rank. Curvature identifies the latter rank with the numerical dimension $\nu(P)$, measured by its nonzero intersection powers. Let $D$ be the reduced boundary where the retained variation has nonidentity local monodromy. Brunebarbe–Cadorel gives $K_S+D$ big (Brunebarbe and Cadorel 2020) <a id="pm-cite-18"></a>[[BrunebarbeCadorel](#ref-BrunebarbeCadorel)]. Using André’s monodromy normality and Bakker–Tsimerman’s Ax–Schanuel theorem to analyze fibers of the line map, the boundary argument proves $\nu(P|_{D_i})<\nu(P)$ for every component $D_i$ of $D$, including finite nonidentity monodromy (André 1992; Bakker and Tsimerman 2019) <a id="pm-cite-19"></a>[[AndreMonodromy](#ref-AndreMonodromy), [BakkerTsimermanAxSchanuel](#ref-BakkerTsimermanAxSchanuel)]. The boundary loss in the asymptotic section count consequently has lower degree in the coefficient of $P$ than the available sections on $S$. Lemma <a href="#period:subtract" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="period:subtract">24</a> turns this comparison into bigness of $K_S+aP$.

Section <a href="#generaltype:section" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="generaltype:section">5</a> proves log-general-type addition in class $\mathcal C$. Kovács–Patakfalvi’s stable-family positivity applies to projective families. We construct such an auxiliary family and descend its relative sections to the original class-$\mathcal C$ base. Fujino’s weak positivity supplies the other direct-image input used in Section <a href="#addition:section" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="addition:section">3</a> (Kovács and Patakfalvi 2017; Fujino 2017). Section <a href="#application:section" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="application:section">6</a> then returns once to the original fibration, applies the addition principle to $(W,T,M)$, and derives the ordinary, logarithmic, characteristic-zero, and core consequences. The core and the relevant specialness of Kodaira-zero fibers are Campana’s constructions and results (Campana 2004, 2011) <a id="pm-cite-20"></a>[[Campana2004](#ref-Campana2004), [CampanaOrbifolds](#ref-CampanaOrbifolds)].

<a id="pm-h-5"></a>
#### The reduced-boundary refinement

For a rational boundary, the root form above spans one character subspace of the cyclic cover’s highest Hodge space; the whole highest space can be larger. For projective reduced-boundary pairs of logarithmic Kodaira dimension zero, Section <a href="#cyc:section" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="cyc:section">7</a> proves more: the minimal root cover has a one-dimensional entire logarithmic top-form space. Mixed-Hodge extension and semipositivity in the forms of Fujino–Fujisawa control the coefficient-one boundary (Fujino and Fujisawa 2014, 2025) <a id="pm-cite-21"></a>[[FujinoFujisawa](#ref-FujinoFujisawa), [FujinoFujisawaVMHS](#ref-FujinoFujisawaVMHS)]. We identify the resulting line with the moduli divisor in the projective subpair setting of Bakker–Filipazzi–Mauri–Tsimerman (Bakker et al. 2025, Theorem 6.28) <a id="pm-cite-22"></a>[[BFMT](#ref-BFMT)]. This identification does not use their separate semiampleness theorem, whose hypotheses include effectiveness over the generic point (Bakker et al. 2025, Theorem 1.5) <a id="pm-cite-23"></a>[[BFMT](#ref-BFMT)]. Section <a href="#cyc:section" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="cyc:section">7</a> also gives the exact divisorial normalization. Appendix <a href="#p1logcbfalt:section" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="p1logcbfalt:section">8</a> collects complementary projective calculations by valuations and toroidal models, together with the comparison on higher models.

The logarithmic lower bound, adjoint positivity, and this reduced-boundary refinement supply the stated inputs to the whole-fiber-variation companion (OpenAI 2026b) <a id="pm-cite-24"></a>[[IitakaVariationCompanion](#ref-IitakaVariationCompanion)]. The reverse-inequality companion (OpenAI 2026d) <a id="pm-cite-25"></a>[[IitakaAdditivityCompanion](#ref-IitakaAdditivityCompanion)] uses the lower bound only for additivity; its upper theorem has additional smoothness hypotheses on all boundary strata. Neither companion is used in the proof of Theorem <a href="#main:orbifold" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="main:orbifold">1</a>. The separate article on projective Hodge lines (OpenAI 2026c) <a id="pm-cite-26"></a>[[IitakaProjectiveCompanion](#ref-IitakaProjectiveCompanion)] gives a complete ordinary proof through rationally polarized integral variations, together with the distinct integral and analytic boundary arguments.

<a id="setup:section"></a>
### The relative Iitaka reduction and its Hodge line

The first task is to replace the fibers by their Iitaka images while retaining every sufficiently divisible pluricanonical system. We first fix the birational models on which divisorial order tests suffice. The root form of a Kodaira-zero fiber will then provide a Hodge line and an exact comparison of sections on the intermediate base.

We use additive notation for rational line bundles; equality of them means equality in $\operatorname{Pic}\otimes\mathbf Q$. A rational line is effective if a positive integral multiple has a nonzero section. All degree assertions below are made after clearing denominators. For a fibration $g:(Z,\Gamma)\to T$ and a very general smooth fiber $F$, adjunction gives <a id="setup:fiber-adjunction"></a>$$\label{setup:fiber-adjunction}
 (K_Z+\Gamma)|_F=K_F+\Gamma|_F.$$

<a id="pm-h-7"></a>
#### The fixed smooth models

<a id="setup:neat-formula"></a>
A smooth-base fibration $g:(Z,\Gamma)\to T$ is *neat* if there is a proper bimeromorphic orbifold morphism $w:(Z,\Gamma)\to(Z_0,\Gamma_0)$ to a smooth pair, with $w_*\Gamma=\Gamma_0$, such that every prime divisor sent by $g$ into codimension at least two is also sent by $w$ into codimension at least two. Campana’s construction gives suitable neat models, model independence, and the formula $$\label{setup:neat-formula}
 \kappa(g\mid\Gamma)=\kappa\bigl(T,K_T+B(g,\Gamma)\bigr)
 \quad\text{on a neat model}$$ (Campana 2011, Definition 3.8, Proposition 3.10, Corollary 4.11, and Remark 6.2) <a id="pm-cite-27"></a>[[CampanaOrbifolds](#ref-CampanaOrbifolds)]. For the proof we only need the defining inequality, valid on every allowed smooth model: <a id="setup:base-comparison"></a>$$\label{setup:base-comparison}
 \kappa\bigl(T,K_T+B(g,\Gamma)\bigr)
 \ \geq\ \kappa(g\mid\Gamma).$$

<a id="pm-h-8"></a>
#### Reduced exceptional boundaries

<a id="stmt-setup-log-modification"></a>
<div id="setup:log-modification" class="lemma">

<a id="setup:log-discrepancy"></a>
<a id="setup:section-invariance"></a>
**Lemma 2** (Logarithmic modifications). *Let $p:Z'\to Z$ be a proper bimeromorphic holomorphic map between smooth compact manifolds. Suppose that $\Gamma$ has simple normal crossing support and coefficients in $\mathbf Q\cap[0,1]$, and set $$\Gamma'=p_*^{-1}\Gamma+\operatorname{Exc}(p)_{\mathrm{red}}.$$ Assume, after further resolution if necessary, that this boundary has simple normal crossing support. There is an effective $p$-exceptional rational divisor $A$ such that $$\label{setup:log-discrepancy}
 K_{Z'}+\Gamma'=p^*(K_Z+\Gamma)+A.$$ For every sufficiently divisible positive integer $m$, the natural identification of meromorphic pluricanonical forms induces $$\label{setup:section-invariance}
 p_*\mathcal O_{Z'}\bigl(m(K_{Z'}+\Gamma')\bigr)
 =\mathcal O_Z\bigl(m(K_Z+\Gamma)\bigr).$$ These identifications respect multiplication, so the divisible section rings and their Iitaka dimensions agree. Moreover, $p$ satisfies <a href="#setup:orbifold-morphism" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="setup:orbifold-morphism">[setup:orbifold-morphism]</a>.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-setup-log-modification"><summary>Proof of stmt-setup-log-modification</summary>

*Proof.* The difference in <a href="#setup:log-discrepancy" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="setup:log-discrepancy">[setup:log-discrepancy]</a> is exceptional, since $p$ is an isomorphism at the generic point of every target prime. To compute its coefficient at an exceptional prime $E$, choose compatible local canonical forms. Near a general point of its center on $Z$, let $z_1,\ldots,z_n$ be coordinates in which the boundary components containing that center are $z_i=0$ for $1\leq i\leq r$, with coefficients $\gamma_i$. Put $t_i=\mathop{\mathrm{ord}}_E(p^*z_i)$ and let $k_E$ be the order of the Jacobian of $p$ along $E$. The pullback of $$\frac{dz_1}{z_1}\wedge\cdots\wedge\frac{dz_r}{z_r}
 \wedge dz_{r+1}\wedge\cdots\wedge dz_n$$ has at most a simple pole along $E$. Indeed, at a general smooth point of $E$, each logarithmic factor has the form $t_i\,du/u$ plus a holomorphic one-form, and a nonzero wedge product contains at most one factor $du/u$. Consequently $k_E+1\geq\sum_i t_i$. The coefficient in question is therefore $$k_E+1-\sum_i\gamma_i t_i
 \ \geq\ \sum_i(1-\gamma_i)t_i\ \geq\ 0.$$ For an effective integral exceptional divisor $mA$, normality of $Z$ gives $p_*\mathcal O_{Z'}(mA)=\mathcal O_Z$: a meromorphic function with poles only on $mA$ descends outside the codimension-two exceptional image and extends by Hartogs’ theorem. The projection formula proves <a href="#setup:section-invariance" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="setup:section-invariance">[setup:section-invariance]</a>, compatibly with products and ratios.

Finally, an exceptional prime has infinite boundary multiplicity. A nonexceptional prime is a strict transform, with pullback order one and unchanged coefficient. These two cases prove <a href="#setup:orbifold-morphism" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="setup:orbifold-morphism">[setup:orbifold-morphism]</a>. ◻

</details>

<a id="stmt-setup-exceptional-descent"></a>
<div id="setup:exceptional-descent" class="corollary">

**Corollary 3** (Descent across exceptional centers). *In the situation of [setup:log-modification](#setup:log-modification), a meromorphic $m$-pluricanonical form on $Z'$ satisfying the boundary bounds of $m\Gamma'$ at every nonexceptional prime descends to a section of $m(K_Z+\Gamma)$ whenever $m\Gamma$ and $m\Gamma'$ are integral. The assertion also holds after tensoring by the pullback of a line bundle on $Z$.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-setup-exceptional-descent"><summary>Proof of stmt-setup-exceptional-descent</summary>

*Proof.* On the complement of the exceptional image the form is a holomorphic section of the prescribed line bundle on $Z$: divisorial regularity suffices on a smooth space. A local trivialization reduces extension across the remaining codimension-two analytic subset to Hartogs’ extension theorem. The tensor-twisted assertion has the same proof. ◻

</details>

<a id="stmt-setup-modification-compatibility"></a>
<div id="setup:modification-compatibility" class="corollary">

**Corollary 4** (Fibers, composition, and a fixed base). *Let $p:(Z',\Gamma')\to(Z,\Gamma)$ be a modification with the boundary rule of [setup:log-modification](#setup:log-modification). Then:*

1.  *If $p$ is a modification over a fixed base, it induces a logarithmic modification on a very general smooth fiber. The log Kodaira dimensions of the corresponding fibers agree.*

2.  *A finite composition of such modifications again has boundary equal to the strict transform of the initial boundary plus the reduced exceptional divisor of the composite.*

3.  *If $g:(Z,\Gamma)\to T$ has smooth base and $g'=g\circ p$, then <a id="setup:fixed-base-boundary"></a>$$\label{setup:fixed-base-boundary}
     B(g',\Gamma')=B(g,\Gamma).$$*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-setup-modification-compatibility"><summary>Proof of stmt-setup-modification-compatibility</summary>

*Proof.* For (i), choose the parameter so that the relevant smoothness and dimension statements hold for the finitely many exceptional components and their images. A component dominating the base restricts to a reduced divisor, and its exceptional image restricts to codimension at least two. Components not dominating the base do not meet this fiber. The restricted map has the same boundary rule, so [setup:log-modification](#setup:log-modification) applies on the fiber. For (ii), every prime exceptional for the composite is either newly exceptional or the transform of an earlier exceptional prime; in both cases its coefficient is one.

For (iii), fix a prime $D\subset T$. Every old prime dominating $D$ has a strict transform, with the same pullback order and boundary multiplicity. Every new prime in the minimum defining $m(g',\Gamma';D)$ is exceptional for $p$, so its boundary multiplicity is infinite. Adding these entries leaves the old minimum unchanged, including when that minimum was already infinite. ◻

</details>

For choice independence in the singular-base definition, take a common resolution of two chosen diagrams. Their induced source boundaries coincide: old primes retain their coefficients and all primes exceptional over the initial $X$ have coefficient one. The comparison maps satisfy [setup:log-modification](#setup:log-modification), so the smooth fibrations are equivalent and their total-space and very general fiber log Kodaira dimensions agree.

<a id="pm-h-9"></a>
#### Flattening relative to a fixed reference pair

To compare forms over generic points of base divisors, we need control of source divisors over higher codimension. Proper analytic flattening gives a base modification whose strict transform is flat (Hironaka 1975; Fujiki 1978) <a id="pm-cite-28"></a>[[HironakaFlattening](#ref-HironakaFlattening), [FujikiDouady](#ref-FujikiDouady)]. Here the strict transform is the closure of the original family over the isomorphism locus, with base torsion removed. Further base change preserves flatness. We use only the resulting equidimensionality before resolving this intermediate source.

<a id="stmt-setup-flattening"></a>
<div id="setup:flattening" class="lemma">

**Lemma 5** (Flattening and the exceptional reference). *Let $g_0:(Z_0,\Gamma_0)\to T_0$ be a fibration from a smooth compact pair with rational SNC boundary in $[0,1]$ to a smooth compact manifold. There are a smooth-base modification $q:T\to T_0$, a smooth-source modification $p:Z\to Z_0$, and a fibration $g:Z\to T$ with $q\circ g=g_0\circ p$ such that, for $$\Gamma=p_*^{-1}\Gamma_0+\operatorname{Exc}(p)_{\mathrm{red}},$$ the boundary has simple normal crossing support and every prime divisor $E\subset Z$ satisfying $\operatorname{codim}_Tg(E)\geq2$ is $p$-exceptional. In particular every such prime has coefficient one in $\Gamma$, and the resulting fibration is neat with reference pair $(Z_0,\Gamma_0)$.*

*One may resolve prescribed proper analytic bad loci on the base during this construction. After any finite sequence of further base modifications, one can choose dominating smooth source models for which the same exceptional assertion holds with respect to the original reference $Z_0$.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-setup-flattening"><summary>Proof of stmt-setup-flattening</summary>

*Proof.* Flatten first, before resolving the source. After resolving the new base by further base change, denote the equidimensional strict transform by $\bar g:\bar Z\to T$, and its modification to $Z_0$ by $\bar p$. Resolve $\bar Z$ and all boundary data, and write $$Z\xrightarrow{r}\bar Z\xrightarrow{\bar p}Z_0,
 \qquad p=\bar p\circ r,
 \qquad g=\bar g\circ r.$$ Let $N=\dim Z_0$, $b=\dim T$, and $d=N-b$. If a prime $E$ on $Z$ has image contained in an analytic subset $C\subset T$ of codimension at least two, equidimensionality gives $$\dim\bar g^{-1}(C)\leq\dim C+d\leq N-2.$$ It follows that $r(E)$, and therefore also $p(E)$, has dimension at most $N-2$. Thus $E$ is exceptional over $Z_0$. The boundary and orbifold-morphism assertions follow from [setup:log-modification](#setup:log-modification). The new map has connected general fiber. Its Stein factorization has a finite bimeromorphic map to the normal base $T$, which is an isomorphism, so all its fibers are connected.

For any further base modification $T'\to T$, pull back the flat intermediate family and take a common resolution dominating the previous smooth source. The same dimension estimate proves exceptionality over $Z_0$. This includes principalizations of prescribed bad loci and their SNC resolutions on the base. Iterating proves the persistence assertion; <span class="upright">(ii)</span> identifies the stepwise boundary with that computed directly over $Z_0$. ◻

</details>

The resolution need not be flat: the conclusion we retain is exceptionality over $Z_0$, which permits [setup:exceptional-descent](#setup:exceptional-descent).

All constructions remain available in Fujiki class $\mathcal C$. A smooth compact member admits a compact Kähler modification (Demailly and Păun 2004, Theorems 0.7 and 3.4) <a id="pm-cite-29"></a>[[DemaillyPaun2004](#ref-DemaillyPaun2004)]. Starting with one for $Z_0$, graph resolutions and principalizations by smooth-center blowups give a Kähler dominating source. Kählerness is preserved by these projective modifications, and proper meromorphic images remain in class $\mathcal C$ (Fujiki 1978, Lemmas 4.4 and 4.6<span class="upright">(3)</span>) <a id="pm-cite-30"></a>[[FujikiDouady](#ref-FujikiDouady)]. Thus the bases also admit Kähler models. The preceding dimension argument still refers to the original $Z_0$.

<a id="pm-h-10"></a>
#### Keeping the original base fixed

<a id="stmt-setup-simultaneous"></a>
<div id="setup:simultaneous" class="corollary">

**Corollary 6** (Simultaneous model control). *Suppose a smooth pair $(X_1,\Delta_1)$ has a fibration $f_1:X_1\to Y$ to a fixed smooth base, and every prime divisor contracted by $f_1$ into codimension at least two has coefficient one in $\Delta_1$. Put $C=B(f_1,\Delta_1)$. Let a factorization through another smooth compact space be given, after an allowed source modification if necessary, by $$X_1\xrightarrow{g_1}W_1\xrightarrow{h_1}Y.$$ Apply [setup:flattening](#setup:flattening) to $g_1$ and make any finite number of subsequent modifications of $W_1$, always taking dominating smooth source models with reduced added exceptional boundaries. The resulting diagram $X\xrightarrow{g}W\xrightarrow{h}Y$, with source boundary $\Delta$, can be chosen to have all the following properties:*

1.  *Every prime contracted by $g$ into codimension at least two is exceptional over the chosen smooth reference source for $g_1$.*

2.  *Every prime contracted by $f=h\circ g$ into codimension at least two in $Y$ has coefficient one in $\Delta$.*

<a id="setup:retained-multiplicity"></a>
3.  *$B(f,\Delta)=C$. In particular, for each prime $D\subset Y$ and each prime $E\subset X$ dominating $D$, $$\label{setup:retained-multiplicity}
     \mathop{\mathrm{ord}}_E(f^*D)\,m_\Delta(E)\geq m_C(D).$$*

4.  *The log Kodaira dimensions of the total source and of its very general fibers over $Y$ are unchanged by these source modifications.*

*The hypothesis on contracted divisors follows from [setup:flattening](#setup:flattening), applied to the original map to its resolved base before fixing $Y$.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-setup-simultaneous"><summary>Proof of stmt-setup-simultaneous</summary>

*Proof.* Assertion (i) is [setup:flattening](#setup:flattening). For (ii), a source prime is either newly exceptional, with coefficient one, or the strict transform of an old prime with unchanged image in $Y$. The hypothesis handles the latter case. Assertions (iii) and (iv) are ; <a href="#setup:retained-multiplicity" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="setup:retained-multiplicity">[setup:retained-multiplicity]</a> is the defining minimum for $C$. Flattening the original map supplies the initial coefficient-one condition by making its contracted primes exceptional. ◻

</details>

In applications we include the critical values and images of boundary strata among the base bad loci, so that $C$ has SNC support. We then fix $Y$ and use [setup:simultaneous](#setup:simultaneous) for changes of intermediate bases.

<a id="pm-h-11"></a>
#### The relative Iitaka map

<a id="stmt-application-relative-iitaka"></a>
<div id="application:relative-iitaka" class="lemma">

**Lemma 7** (Relative Iitaka construction). *Let $f:(X,\Delta)\to Y$ be a fibration between smooth compact manifolds in Fujiki class $\mathcal C$, with $\Delta$ a rational SNC boundary in $[0,1]$. Suppose that $$k=\kappa(F,K_F+\Delta_F)\geq 0$$ on very general smooth fibers. After modifications of the source, adding the reduced exceptional divisor to the strict transform of the boundary, there is a factorization $$X\xrightarrow{g}W\xrightarrow{h}Y$$ with smooth compact intermediate space in class $\mathcal C$, both maps proper with connected fibers, and $$\dim W_y=k,\qquad
 \kappa(G,K_G+\Delta_G)=0$$ for very general fibers of $h$ and $g$, respectively. The factorization restricts to the log Iitaka fibration on very general $f$-fibers. These properties persist on higher models obtained by the same rule for the source boundary.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-application-relative-iitaka"><summary>Proof of stmt-application-relative-iitaka</summary>

*Proof.* Consider all degrees clearing the denominators of $\Delta$. Their relative adjoint direct images are coherent. First shrink to the dense analytic open where the map and boundary strata are smooth. There the family and its adjoint line bundles are flat over the base; generic constancy of the fiber section dimension permits cohomology and base change (Grauert 1960, sec. 7, nos. 4–6, Sätze 3–5) <a id="pm-cite-31"></a>[[GrauertDirectImages](#ref-GrauertDirectImages)], with its corrigendum (Grauert 1963) <a id="pm-cite-32"></a>[[GrauertDirectImagesCorr](#ref-GrauertDirectImagesCorr)]. Together with the evaluation ranks this gives a dense analytic open for each degree; outside the resulting countable collection of exceptional sets, the maximum image dimension is $k$, attained in one fixed degree.

Choose a sufficiently divisible degree giving the stabilized maps, resolve the relative meromorphic map to the projective bundle of its direct image, and take Stein factorization onto the image. The Iitaka fibration theorem for line bundles on compact complex spaces identifies the resulting map on very general fibers (Ueno 1975, vol. 439, chap. II, §5; Chapter III, §7) <a id="pm-cite-33"></a>[[UenoClassification](#ref-UenoClassification)]. No finite generation is required. The image has relative dimension $k$; its Stein factor is in class $\mathcal C$, being a proper image of a space in that class. Resolve it and the resulting source map. Connectedness of the fibers of $h$ follows from that of $f$, since their images under $g$ are precisely the fibers of $h$.

The assertion that the restriction has Kodaira dimension zero concerns the original line bundle. Its standard proof applies here: coherent direct images on the projective Iitaka image acquire sections after an ample twist. If the restriction had positive Iitaka dimension, multiplying those sections by powers of the defining system would enlarge the Iitaka image. A nonzero defining section restricts nontrivially to a very general Iitaka fiber, so the restriction has dimension exactly zero.

Apply this to the resolved log adjoint on a very general $f$-fiber. Its divisible section ring is unchanged by [setup:log-modification](#setup:log-modification), and adjunction identifies the restriction with $K_G+\Delta_G$. Further source modifications preserve the same section ring, hence the relative Iitaka factorization and its asserted fiber dimensions. ◻

</details>

<a id="rankone:section"></a>
#### The exact comparison for Kodaira-zero fibers

For fibers of logarithmic Kodaira dimension zero, the relative pluricanonical forms determine a single Hodge line on the base. We construct that line, compute its boundary orders, and use them to identify the adjoint section spaces. Coefficient-one boundary components introduce mixed Hodge structures, but the line itself lies in one pure weight grade.

A pure variation of Hodge structures carries a local system and a varying Hodge filtration. The *highest step* of a summand is its highest nonzero filtration piece. The *parabolic extension* of such a line is obtained by local power substitutions making boundary monodromy unipotent, extension of the unipotent Hodge filtration, and descent with the resulting rational weights. We will compute those weights using the actual relative pluricanonical form.

<a id="stmt-rankone-comparison"></a>
<div id="rankone:comparison" class="proposition">

**Proposition 8** (The rank-one comparison). *Let $g:(X,\Delta)\to W$ be a fibration with connected fibers from a smooth compact Kähler manifold to a smooth compact manifold in class $\mathcal C$. Suppose that $\Delta$ is an SNC boundary with rational coefficients in $[0,1]$ and that $\kappa(G,K_G+\Delta_G)=0$ on a very general smooth fiber $G$. After the modifications of [setup:log-modification](#setup:log-modification), [setup:flattening](#setup:flattening), fix a smooth compact reference pair $(X_0,\Delta_0)$ and a modification $$\pi:X\longrightarrow X_0,\qquad
 \Delta=\pi_*^{-1}\Delta_0+\operatorname{Exc}(\pi)_{\mathrm{red}},$$ such that every prime divisor mapped by $g$ into codimension at least two is $\pi$-exceptional. Then there are a rational line bundle $M$ and a rational divisor $T$ on $W$ with the following properties.*

1.  *A positive integral multiple of $M$ is the parabolic highest Hodge line of a complex direct summand of a pure, real-polarizable variation with an integral lattice on a dense open subset of $W$. Its parabolic extension is the line to which the adjoint-positivity theorem, [period:adjoint](#period:adjoint), will apply.*

<a id="rankone:boundary"></a>
2.  *The divisor $T$ is supported on an SNC divisor and satisfies $$\label{rankone:boundary}
